touches 1.0. Do not quote "13% slower" as a measured fact.

The single DFlash2 win, `beer-song`, is simultaneously the most repetitive output and the
longest prompt, so those two explanations are not separable by this data.

**Wiring facts that cost real time to learn.** `--spec-type draft-mtp` reads the MTP head out
of the *target* GGUF — the standalone `MTP/mtp-*.gguf` is never loaded. `-md` alone never
speculates (`docs/speculative.md:71`); without `--spec-type` you measure autoregressive decode
and get a large false regression. `--spec-draft-n-max 8` against DFlash2's 8-token block
clamps to 7 (`common/speculative.cpp:986-995`), which is why upstream documents 7.

**Overturned by:** a leg across languages and prompt lengths, or a cheaper DFlash2 drafter.
Note upstream's own 3090 figures (62.21 vs 57.79 tok/s) claim the opposite sign.

---

## The engine: llama.cpp, not the patched vLLM fork

Four levers against the fork's prefill deficit, all null or worse: prefix caching on (median
1.818x slower), `MAX_SEQS=1` (no change), MTP (block 864 -> 832 only), and `CTX=huge` with
4/2-bit KV (`rust/say` took 1,116 s against llama.cpp's 80 s, with 6x the output tokens
because the KV quantization degraded coherence).

The block-granularity hypothesis for the poor prefix-cache hit rate is **refuted, not
confirmed**: the one arm that gave `block_size=128` saw the hit rate *fall*, 75% -> 37%.

**Not settled:** stock upstream vLLM on an unmodified checkpoint has never been measured
(`results/session1/HANDOFF.md:603`), and the fork comparison admits quant and action-trace
confounding.

---

## Context and KV: 131,072 at q8_0, one slot

Measured on the box: 22,758 MiB of 24,576 with MTP live. This **clears the >120k rule**; it
is not established as the ceiling — the repo's own fit artifacts cannot distinguish "ceiling"
from "at least 131,072" (`README.md:145`).

KV evidence is q8_0 against f16 on three short JavaScript prompts only. It does not settle
long-context quality, and q8_0 is not shown optimal.

`--ctx-size` is a *total* KV budget split across slots: `PARALLEL=2` gives each agent half.
At 120k per agent a 3090 serves one Q4 agent.

---

## Renting versus API

**Local, measured:** run 13, 78 tasks, 81.6% GPU busy, 531,751 output tokens over 14,155 s.
At $0.197/hr that is **$1.46 per M output tokens**, or ~$1.67/M counting the full rental
window rather than the leg. Input is free at an hourly rate, and agentic coding runs 33:1
input:output — combined, $0.04/M. That ratio, not decode speed, is why local competes.

**SUPERSEDED 2026-08-28 — the API is now the expensive side.** The $0.0049/task below was
computed from list prices that have since risen; `deepseek-v4-flash` is $0.087/$0.017/$0.174
per M (in/cache-read/out) against the pinned `-0731` snapshot's $0.060/$0.012/$0.120, about
45% higher. Recomputed against **our own measured token usage** on the same 78-task set
(`results/session2/api-*/polyglot.jsonl`, token counts only — the rows never carried prices,
so any rate can be re-applied):

| lane | $/task | PLN/task | fresh in | cache read | out |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DeepSeek V4 Flash | 0.0163 | 0.065 | 95,865 | 336,309 | 12,978 |
| GPT-5.6 Luna | 0.0181 | 0.072 | 29,884 | 262,820 | 5,682 |
| GLM 5.3 Flash | 0.0189 | 0.076 | 73,208 | 820,175 | 4,490 |
| **Qwen3.8-27B via API** | **0.1517** | **0.607** | 266,881 | 175,964 | 9,154 |
| 2x3060 electricity, Warsaw | 0.0065 | 0.026 | | | |
| RTX 3090 electricity, Warsaw | 0.0049 | 0.020 | | | |

Local electricity is ~2.5x cheaper per task than the cheapest API. **Renting Qwen3.8-27B
costs 23x running it yourself** — that fills the "has NOT been costed" gap, on 9 rows.

Per-task cost conflates price with verbosity: GLM pulls 893k prompt tokens/task against
Luna's 292k, so this ranks *models on this workload*, not rate cards. Assumes
`reasoning_tokens` sit inside `completion_tokens`; if billed separately, Flash rises ~$0.0018.
Electricity at 1.10 PLN/kWh and 350 W wall — both estimates.

**Renting still beats buying.** A rented 3090 is $0.0099/task against $0.0065 owning two
3060s: 1500 PLN amortises over ~110,000 tasks, about six years at fifty a day.

Superseded text: DeepSeek V4 Flash at $0.0049/task against $0.0050 renting a 5070 Ti and
$0.0063–0.0099 on a 3090; break-even at ~514,000 tasks.

**A remote Qwen3.8-27B endpoint has NOT been costed.** An earlier "~13x cheaper local"
figure was withdrawn: the $1.6863 it rested on was spent by an invocation that recorded zero
tasks, and the 9 rows that exist came from a second invocation with no closing balance.

So the case for local does **not** rest on cost. It rests on capability — and that is the
one thing still unmeasured.

---

- **Capability.** SWE-bench has never run. Whether Qwen3.8-27B is nearer Flash or Pro decides
  whether any of the above matters. Blocked on `php`, `composer`, `gradle`, `bundle` missing
  from the generation host, which floors ~18 of 52 instances.
- **Batching, the cheapest untested lever.** `serving/serve.sh` hard-codes `--parallel 1`, so
  every number in this repo is single-stream. Decode is memory-bound, so two concurrent
  sequences reuse the same weight reads and *aggregate* throughput should nearly double while
  per-agent speed barely moves. `PARALLEL` is already plumbed; nobody has ever measured it.
  This may deliver most of what a third and fourth card would buy, for the price of one leg.
- ~~**32 GB.** No arm.~~ **Closed 2026-08-29.** `results/session2/split-mode-5060ti` is a
  32 GB arm: two 5060 Ti 16GB under `-sm tensor` load 131,072 at 63.10 tok/s using 12,012
  MiB of 16,311 per card, so 32 GB is not context-limited at all.
- ~~**The 2060 Super's fate.**~~ **Closed 2026-08-31: pair it.** 32,768 binds; the working
  floor is 90,000-100,000. The pairing wins on the metric this repo actually uses. Tokens
  to solution is `token_cost / decode`, so Q4_K_XL paired reads 1.00/40.2 = 0.0249 against
  single-card IQ3_XXS at 1.66-2.02/66.2 = 0.0251-0.0305 -- **the pairing is 1.01x to 1.23x
  faster to a solution while also being the quality baseline and reaching 131,072 rather
  than 106,496.** Same shape as the IQ3_XXS rejection above: the faster-decoding quant
  loses on tokens. Because the floor is 90-100k and not 131,072, the 2060 Super may also
  drive the display -- that costs ~1 GiB and lands near 117,600, still clear. Cost: an
  `sm_75;sm_120` build, an explicit `--tensor-split`, 340 W, and prefill of 350-450.
  Treat it as a bridge to a second 5060 Ti, not a destination: CUDA 13 dropped Maxwell,
  Pascal and Volta, so `sm_75` is now the minimum supported architecture.
- **The 2x3060 context ceiling.** Whether `--no-mmproj` and `-fit off` recover 131,072, and
  where the true ceiling sits between 106,496 and 131,072. One rental, about $0.20.
- **Power ceiling versus throughput.** Not measurable on rented hardware: `nvidia-smi -pl`
  returns `Insufficient Permissions` in a vast.ai container (`results/session2/power-sweep-2gpu`).
  The card's own range was recovered before the abort -- **100 W floor, 170 W stock, 212 W
  ceiling** -- so 60 percent of stock is within 2 W of the floor and the sweep is available
  on owned hardware. Untested, and the expectation that memory-bound decode barely degrades
  is physics plus general report, not a measurement of this configuration.
  Renting a host whose cards are *already* throttled was tried as a proxy and abandoned:
  `POWERMAX`/`LANESMIN` were added to `box.sh` and work, but the slice that satisfies
  <=130 W **and** x16 **and** CUDA >= 13.0 holds zero to two offers at any moment and they
  are taken within minutes. Three launches found no offer; one landed on machine 148558,
  which is quarantined for a genuine Docker fault. The proxy is also confounded -- a
  different host differs in CPU, cooling and lanes -- so it was never worth much.
- ~~**Tensor split attribution on our own harness.**~~ **Closed 2026-08-29.** The 16 GB pair
  ran both modes at matched context: `-sm tensor` 64.17/65.03 decode against `-sm layer`
  41.20/41.39, a 1.56x effect, with prefill going the other way (643-687 against 653-663).
  That is the expected physics -- decode aggregates memory buses, prefill pays the per-layer
  synchronisation tensor split imposes.
- **Why 2x5060 Ti out-decodes a 3090.** Acceptance rate and power throttling are ruled out
  above. Distinguishing GDDR7 bandwidth efficiency from Blackwell verify throughput needs a
  no-MTP arm on both cards, which isolates raw decode from the speculative path.
- **Q3 versus Q4 at adequate power.** n=150 would resolve what n=40 could not.
- **Stock upstream vLLM.**
- **The cold `./serve` path**, never run end to end as currently written.


# qwen38-eval

A harness for measuring local LLM serving stacks: engine × quant × decoding config × GPU,
graded on real coding tasks rather than on throughput alone.

Built for one purchase decision — does low-bit quantization of **Qwen3.8-27B** damage
coding ability enough to force a 24–32 GB GPU instead of a 16 GB one? — and being
generalized from it. Session 1's answer, its evidence and its wrong turns are in
`results/session1/`.

Codex drives an OpenAI-compatible server over an SSH tunnel to a rented GPU. One row is
emitted per task with full telemetry: tokens, timings, cache, context depth, tool calls.

## Start here

Local default: **paired Swift IQ4_XS**, daily context 90,112, MTP on.
Use the existing `~/.codex/skills/local-qwen-server/bin/serve.sh start`
(the Claude copy has identical serving behavior). Use
`PROFILE=swift-headless .../serve.sh start` for the measured 102,400-token profile,
or stop before switching to `PROFILE=iq3-single .../serve.sh start` for the preserved
single-5060 IQ3 fallback.
The engine remains `~/repos/llama.cpp/build/bin/llama-server`.
See [next experiments](results/session2/pair-5060ti-2060s/NEXT-EXPERIMENTS.md)
for the current bounded tuning plan and client-context requirements in the skill.

| file | what it is |
|---|---|
| `results/session1/STATUS.md` | **live numbers**, regenerated from artifacts; never hand-edited |
| `results/session1/HANDOFF.md` | what was measured, what it means, and every trap already paid for |
| `scripts/README.md` | per-script reference: flags, contracts, invocation |

## The layer rule

Shell owns the control plane. Python owns measurement. The boundary is **not language, it
is who may write a number** — shell may launch instruments and hand them facts only it
knows, but must never write a measurement, a label, or a table.

That criterion is not aesthetic. Every mislabeled or lost datum in session 1 occurred where
shell held data the row schema wanted: the GPU column is null in all 192 task rows and
holds rental labels like `qwen38-2x5070ti-ttl4` in 36 throughput rows, a KV-ceiling
measurement survives only as a log line, and `fit.txt` is a space-separated text sink. The
strict, tested Python schema layer never produced a bad row.

The refinement worth keeping: shell may *produce* facts by controlling servers — whether a
context rung loads is a shell measurement and belongs there — but it may not serialize them
into a sink.

| layer | language | owns | files |
|---|---|---|---|
| provider | sh | rent, destroy, ssh routes, offer filtering, TTL labels | `scripts/infra/box.sh`, `scripts/infra/reaper.sh` |
| serving control | sh | launch → wait → *is this the server I asked for* | `scripts/infra/lib.sh` |
| serving | sh, box-side | (engine, flags, quant, ctx) → a running server | `serving/` |
| drivers | sh | one experiment's matrix, and nothing else | `orchestration/session2/polyglot-cost.sh` |
| instruments | py | anything that produces a row | `scripts/instruments/emit.py`, `run_polyglot.py`, `bench_endpoint.py`, `bench_mtp.py`, `longctx.py` |
| analysis | py | anything that produces a table | `scripts/analysis/paired_runs.py`, `analyze_paired.py`, `engine_table.py`, `status_report.py` |
| corpora | py | task selection, dedup, leakage proof | `scripts/corpora/make_subsets.py`, `build_kld_corpora.py`, `selftest_polyglot.py` |
| deliverable | sh | rent a box, serve the model, hand back an endpoint | `./serve` -> `orchestration/serve-box.sh` |
| provenance | sh | what was actually run, kept as a record | `orchestration/archive/` |

**The provider seam is transport, not ssh weirdness.** `box.sh` holds what is true of
vast.ai and nothing else. The ssh-255 tri-state in `lib.sh` looks like a vast quirk and is
not: OpenSSH returns 255 for any client-side failure anywhere, so filing it under the
provider would bury a general serving guard where a non-vast experiment never looks.

`orchestration/` holds one interface and one archive. `serve-box.sh` is the interface --
run it as `./serve` -- and `archive/` is 33 single-use chains that name their own session;
do not copy patterns from those without checking `HANDOFF.md` for which were later found
broken. `orchestration/session2/` sits between the two: parameterised drivers that can be
re-run.

## Sinks

Results are append-only JSONL, one sink per instrument shape.

| sink | shape | written by |
|---|---|---|
| `results/*/codegen/polyglot.jsonl`, `results/*/speed/*-polyglot.jsonl` | 59-field task row | `run_polyglot.py` via `emit.py` |
| `results/*/speed/engines.jsonl` | 12-field throughput row | `bench_endpoint.py` |
| `results/*/{mtp,speed}/*.jsonl` | 19-field speculation row | `bench_mtp.py` |

**Identity belongs in the row, not the path.** A sink named `3090-polyglot.jsonl` whose
`gpu` field is null has put the answer somewhere no analysis can read, and it stops working
the moment two cards write to one file. Session 1 did this; see the reuse gaps below.

`results/` is tracked deliberately: it is the only copy of GPU time that was paid for.

## Running

```bash
python3 -m unittest discover -s tests -q          # 211 tests, stdlib only
PYTHONPATH=. python3 -m scripts.instruments.run_polyglot --help
PYTHONPATH=. python3 -m scripts.analysis.status_report       # refresh STATUS.md
PYTHONPATH=. python3 -m scripts.analysis.paired_runs --base 3 --cand 6  # cost, per task
PYTHONPATH=. python3 -m scripts.analysis.engine_table         # cross-engine throughput
```

A leg is resumable: `--resume` skips tasks already recorded for that quant and run-index,
so a crash costs one task rather than the leg. A new configuration needs a **new**
`--run-index`; reusing a spent one silently records nothing.

## Two rules that decide whether a number is real

**A benchmark can silently measure nothing.** Exercism disables every test after the first,
so a bare `cargo test` exits 0 on an untouched stub — every model would have scored 100%.
Runners refuse to score without a self-test report proving the reference passes *and the
stub fails*. `--skip-selftest` exists for development and marks its rows unverified.

**Pass/fail saturates; the graded instrument is cost to a verified solution.** IQ3_XXS
scores 40/40 on this subset, so a tie proves nothing. Compare outcomes with
`analyze_paired.py` (discordant pairs) and cost with `paired_runs.py` (tokens and wall time
per task) — never with unpaired means, because task difficulty dominates the variance.

**Read the sign test before the ratio.** Costs are heavy-tailed, so even a paired *mean*
follows the one task that ran away: Q3 against Q4 reads 1.59× by mean and 1.28× by
geometric mean, with 23 of 39 tasks costlier — a coin flip. `paired_runs.py` prints all
of them side by side, plus the paired-task count each effect size needs.

Throughput predicts neither. Measured on one 3090, the stack that wins tok/s by 2.4× takes
**2.01× longer per solved task**.

## Recording the environment

Every instrument requires `--env-json`, a capture from `scripts/instruments/gpuenv.py`:

```bash
ssh <box> python3 - < scripts/instruments/gpuenv.py > env.json     # runs on the box, stdlib only
```

**Capture it per leg, not per box.** The power cap is mutable via `nvidia-smi -pl` and is
itself an experiment variable, so a per-box capture would stamp a whole wattage sweep with
its first cap. `cuda_toolkit` comes from `nvcc`; `nvidia-smi`'s "CUDA Version" is the
driver's supported API level, not the installed toolkit.

A local lane refuses to run without it, the same way the server probe refuses to record
when it cannot confirm the served model — a leg whose rows cannot name the card is not
worth the GPU time. `--dry-run` is exempt, being the no-GPU harness check. There is
deliberately no `--gpu` flag: hand-typing the card is how 36 of 124 throughput rows came to
hold a rental label, TTL included, which the table then rendered as the card.

## Known gaps before reusing this

Recorded rather than fixed. Evidence in `results/session1/HANDOFF.md`.

- **No config identity.** `run_index` is a bare int whose meaning lives outside the data;
  `label` packs engine/quant/config in four different conventions. Pairing legs requires
  knowing the mapping from memory.
- **The serving layer is box-side and unversioned.** Recovered under
  `results/session1/rental/serving/`, but it is the component a flag-comparison experiment
  actually varies, and it died with every earlier box.
- **The 33 `orchestration/archive/` scripts do not use the libraries and never will.** They
  are frozen provenance, and 18 of them still call `bench_endpoint.py --gpu`, a flag that no
  longer exists. They already could not run — their boxes and forks are gone — so the
  breakage is recorded rather than repaired. New drivers go in `orchestration/session2/`.
- **No experiment namespace mechanism.** `session1` is hard-coded as `OUT=` in 23 scripts.
- **Max context per config is not data.** It survives as `fit.txt` (space-separated) and as
  `FIT` lines in driver logs, so a ceiling of `131072` cannot be told from "at least
  131072" and a refusal cannot be told from "below the smallest rung tested".
- **`run_swebench.py` and `subsets/swebench.jsonl` have never been run** — no artifact
  anywhere in `results/`. The plumbing is now in place (52 instances, a liveness gate,
  `--resume`, and `grade_swebench.py`), but the first leg still needs GPU credit.


# Dual RTX 5060 Ti: second card not visible (2026-09-28)

## Setup

- Board: ASUS ROG STRIX B550-I GAMING, BIOS 4304 (2026-08-19)
- CPU: Ryzen 9 3900X (Zen 2, Matisse). The CPU x16 slot does PCIe Gen4 on B550.
- CPU x16 slot → passive x8/x8 bifurcation board (slot 12 V fed from a CPU
  4-pin connector) → one riser per card:
  - `08:00.0` RTX 5060 Ti: link at 16.0 GT/s (Gen4) x8
  - `09:00.0` RTX 5060 Ti (replaced the RTX 2060 Super): link at 8.0 GT/s (Gen3) x8, because its riser is Gen3-rated
- OS: Pop!_OS, UEFI boot, NVIDIA open kernel module 595.84

## Symptom

`nvidia-smi -L` lists one GPU. `lspci` lists two `10de:2d04` devices, so the
riser, bifurcation, and link all work. The driver fails on the second card:

```
NVRM: osInitNvMapping: *** Cannot attach gpu
NVRM: GPU 0000:09:00.0: RmInitAdapter failed! (0x22:0x56:894)
```

`09:00.0` has its command register at `0000` (memory decode off), and
`lspci -vv` shows its BARs as `[virtual]` / `[disabled]`.

## Root cause

**Above 4G Decoding is disabled**, so the only PCI memory window is 32-bit:

```
pci_bus 0000:00: root bus resource [mem 0xa0000000-0xefffffff window]   # ~1.25 GB
```

The firmware fits both cards in that window at boot. Linux then tries to
reserve space for the Blackwell cards' SR-IOV `VF BAR`s, releases the bridge
windows, and re-packs. After the re-pack, the second card does not fit:

```
pci 0000:00:03.2: bridge window [mem size 0x24000000 64bit pref]: can't assign; no space
pci 0000:09:00.0: BAR 1 [mem size 0x10000000 64bit pref]: failed to assign
```

The 2060 Super has no SR-IOV VF BARs, so the 5060 Ti + 2060 Super pair fit.
Two 5060 Tis do not. (Inference from the log, consistent with the swap
being the only change.)

## Fix

In the BIOS (Del at boot, F7 for Advanced Mode):

1. Advanced → PCI Subsystem Settings → **Above 4G Decoding: Enabled**. This is the fix.
2. Same menu → **Re-Size BAR Support: Auto/Enabled**. Optional, see below.
3. Boot → CSM → **Launch CSM: Disabled**. ReBAR needs pure UEFI. Pop!_OS
   already boots UEFI (`\EFI\Pop_OS-...` in `/proc/cmdline`), so this is safe.
4. Leave the x8/x8 bifurcation setting as is.
5. F10 to save. After boot, check `nvidia-smi -L` for two GPUs.

Fallback if the BIOS change is not possible (untested): add
`pci=realloc=off` to the kernel command line with
`sudo kernelstub -a "pci=realloc=off"`. This keeps the firmware BAR layout,
which was valid.

Verify after the fix:

```sh
nvidia-smi -L
journalctl -k -b | grep -E 'root bus resource|09:00.0: BAR'   # expect a window above 4 GB
nvidia-smi -q | grep -A3 'BAR1 Memory'                        # 16 GB total means ReBAR is active
```

## After the BIOS fix: Xid 79 on the second card (2026-09-28, 09:41 boot)

BIOS changes: CSM off, Above 4G Decoding on, Re-Size BAR on, SR-IOV on.
Result: a 64-bit window `[mem 0x830000000-0x7fffffffff]` exists, and both
cards get 16 GB BAR1 (ReBAR active). `08:00.0` works. `09:00.0` gets its BARs
but drops off the bus while the driver boots its GSP firmware:

```
NVRM: Xid (PCI:0000:09:00): 79, GPU has fallen off the bus.
NVRM: GPU1 tmrGetTimeEx_GH100: NVRM-RC: Consistently Bad TimeLo value ffffffff
```

The address-space failure had hidden this. Before the fix, the driver never
reached GSP boot on that card.

Most likely cause (inference, not yet confirmed): the Gen3-rated riser cannot
carry Gen4. The link falls back to Gen3 at boot, then drops when the driver
changes link speed during init. The 2060 Super is Gen3-only, so it never tried
Gen4 on this riser. Second candidate: 8-pin or bifurcation-board 4-pin power.

Next steps:
1. Force the x16 slot to Gen3 in the BIOS (likely Advanced → Onboard Devices
   Configuration). This probably drops `08:00.0` to Gen3 as well.
2. If it still fails, reseat the 8-pin on card 2 and the 4-pin on the board.
3. If it still fails, swap the risers. A fault that follows the riser points to
   the riser. A fault that stays with the card points to the card or its power.

## Resolution: cap only root port 00:03.2 at Gen3 (2026-09-28, 09:49 boot)

The BIOS "Gen3" option that was changed did not reach the GPU slot: both
root ports still reported `max_link_speed=16.0 GT/s`. Under load, `09:00.0`
stayed at Gen1 x8 (1.4-1.8 GB/s host copies), which supports the riser
explanation: the card downshifts to save power and cannot retrain upward to
Gen4 over the Gen3 riser.

Capping the port from Linux fixed it:

```sh
sudo setpci -s 00:03.2 CAP_EXP+0x30.w=0x0003:000f   # LnkCtl2 Target Link Speed = Gen3
sudo setpci -s 00:03.2 CAP_EXP+0x10.w=0x0020:0020   # LnkCtl Retrain Link
```

Result under load (scratch CUDA memcpy test, 256 MiB pinned copies):

| GPU | Link | Host H2D+D2H | 
|---|---|---|
| `08:00.0` | Gen4 x8 | 14.4-14.5 GB/s |
| `09:00.0` | Gen3 x8 | 7.2 GB/s |

GPU0 to GPU1 copy: 7.1 GB/s. P2P access is `0`, so the copy goes through host
memory. No Xid in this boot.

`setpci` changes are lost on reboot. Install the unit
below to apply them at every boot. The address `00:03.2` holds only
while the slot topology stays the same.

```ini
[Unit]
Description=Cap PCIe root port 00:03.2 (Gen3 riser, 2nd RTX 5060 Ti) at Gen3
Before=nvidia-persistenced.service display-manager.service
After=systemd-udev-settle.service

[Service]
Type=oneshot
ExecStart=/usr/bin/setpci -s 00:03.2 CAP_EXP+0x30.w=0x0003:000f
ExecStart=/usr/bin/setpci -s 00:03.2 CAP_EXP+0x10.w=0x0020:0020

[Install]
WantedBy=multi-user.target
```

## Gen3 riser on the second card

Not a problem for this workload, as long as the link stays clean.

- Gen3 x8 is about 7.9 GB/s. Gen4 x8 is about 15.8 GB/s.
- llama.cpp layer split (`--split-mode layer`) sends only a small activation
  tensor across the GPU boundary per token. Decode speed does not change
  measurably. Prompt processing and model load get slightly slower.
- Row / tensor split (`--split-mode row`) moves much more data per token, so it
  is more sensitive to link speed.
- The 2060 Super is a Gen3 card, so the previous pair already ran that slot at
  Gen3. Link bandwidth for that slot does not change against the old arm.
- `aer_dev_correctable` reads `TOTAL_ERR_COR 0` for both cards, but that is
  not evidence of a clean link: AER is enabled only on root ports `00:07.1` and
  `00:08.1`, not on the GPU ports `00:03.1` / `00:03.2`. See the Xid 79 section.

## Bifurcation-board power

The board's CPU 4-pin feeds the slot 12 V for both cards. A 5060 Ti can take
up to ~66 W from the slot (PCIe spec: 5.5 A at 12 V), so the worst case is
~130 W through that 4-pin. That fits within the rating of a 4-pin EPS
connector, and the 2060 Super drew similar slot power before the swap.
Feed the 4-pin and both cards' 8-pins from the same PSU.

## BAR and ReBAR

- **BAR (Base Address Register):** a window in the CPU's physical address
  space through which the CPU reads and writes the device's memory and
  registers. The firmware and OS must place every BAR in a free address range
  inside the parent bridge window. A 5060 Ti has BAR0 (registers, 64 MB), BAR1
  (VRAM aperture, 256 MB by default), and BAR3 (32 MB), plus SR-IOV VF BARs.
- **Above 4G Decoding:** lets the firmware place 64-bit BARs above the 4 GB
  address limit. Without it, all GPUs must share the ~1.25 GB window below
  4 GB. That is the failure above.
- **ReBAR (Resizable BAR):** a PCIe capability that lets the firmware make
  BAR1 as large as the full VRAM (16 GB), so the CPU can reach all of VRAM
  directly instead of through a 256 MB sliding window. It requires Above 4G
  Decoding, because a 16 GB BAR cannot fit below 4 GB. For CUDA inference the
  gain is small (inference): bulk copies use DMA, not the BAR. Enable it
  anyway since there is no downside on this setup.

## Repo impact

The benchmark scripts and the `local-qwen-server` profile expect the
5060 Ti + 2060 Super pair. A 5060 Ti ×2 setup is a different arm.

## Sources

- ASUS ROG STRIX B550-I BIOS manual: https://dlcdnets.asus.com/pub/ASUS/mb/SocketAM4/ROG_STRIX_B550-I_GAMING/E17670_ROG_STRIX_B550-I_BIOS_manual_EM_WEB.pdf
- ASUS ReBAR guide: https://edgeup.asus.com/2021/guide-how-to-enable-resizable-bar-on-your-asus-powered-gaming-pc/
- BIOS downloads: https://rog.asus.com/motherboards/rog-strix/rog-strix-b550-i-gaming-model/helpdesk_bios/

## Tensor-mode regression on the 5060 Ti x2 pair (2026-09-28, open)

Both cards at 150 W, Swift IQ4_XS, same pinned `llama-server` binary (sha256 `5171d9bb…`)
as the 5060 Ti + 2060 SUPER results, which reached ~450-600 pp / 62-69 tg (MTP, tensor 71,29).

| Run | pp tok/s | tg tok/s |
|---|---:|---:|
| server, tensor 71,29, MTP (512-32k prompts) | 113-132 | 16.7-18.8 |
| server, tensor 50,50, no MTP (4k) | 136 | 12.0 |
| server, tensor 50,50, MTP (4k) | 128 | 19.6 |
| server, tensor 50,50, `GGML_CUDA_ALLREDUCE=none` | 83 | 8.6 |
| server, layer 50,50, MTP (4k) | 765 | 39.1 |
| llama-bench tensor 50,50 (no MTP) | 622-670 | 20 |
| llama-bench layer 50,50 (no MTP) | 850 | 23 |

Layer mode is healthy (it adds capacity, not speed, at batch 1). Tensor mode, which the old
pair used for its speedup, is broken.

Ruled out: link speeds (Gen4 x8 / Gen3 x8 as intended), ASPM (`l1_aspm=0` on both), IOMMU mode
(translated in every boot, including the September benchmark boots), binary changes.

Mapped host-flag round trip (the internal AllReduce's signalling): GPU0 1.36 us, GPU1 2.12 us
in steady state. A short run showed 20 us on GPU1 because it included Gen1 -> Gen3 retraining.

`nsys` on llama-bench tensor tg32: `ggml_cuda_ar_kernel` is 97.4% of GPU time on both GPUs,
avg ~430 us (min 6-8 us); non-AllReduce compute ~48 ms per GPU total. Host thread is blocked in
`cudaEventSynchronize` (8,444 x 245 us), `cudaMemcpyAsync` (3,832 x 400 us) and
`cudaMemcpy2DAsync` (448 x 2.4 ms). Kernel launches are normal (4-5 us).

Bisection, same BIOS (Above 4G, ReBAR, SR-IOV on), 5060 Ti + 2060 SUPER back in: healthy.
Server tensor 71,29 + MTP 639 pp / 57.3 tg; llama-bench tensor tg64 33.3, layer 24.3.
`nsys` AR kernel avg: 2060S pair 15.6 us (5060 Ti) / 96.7 us (2060S), against ~430 us on
both cards of the 5060 Ti pair. BIOS, driver and kernel are ruled out.

### Cause: driver start-up on card 2 before the Gen3 cap (resolved 10:5x)

After swapping back, card 2 hit Xid 79 at boot again. Recovery: apply the cap, then
`remove` 09:00.0/09:00.1 in sysfs and `rescan`, so the driver started the card with Gen3
already enforced. Afterwards tensor mode is fast:

| llama-bench tensor 50,50 | pp2048 | tg64 |
|---|---:|---:|
| cap applied after driver start (morning) | 622-670 | 20 |
| cap applied before driver start | 947 | 40.9 |
| same, `GGML_CUDA_AR_COPY_THRESHOLD=0` | 920 | 41.0 |
| 5060 Ti + 2060S, 71,29 | 732 | 33.3 |

Inference: a driver start-up on a link that bounces between Gen1 and failed Gen4 attempts
leaves card 2 in a degraded state that the later `setpci` retrain does not undo. The exact
driver state is not observable here; the before/after is.

Consequence: the systemd unit above runs too late. The cap must be applied before the
`nvidia` module loads (modprobe.d `install` hook), or replace the Gen3 riser with a Gen4 one.
Without either, every boot risks Xid 79 or a silently slow tensor mode.

### Smoke: tensor split 71,29 vs 50,50 on the 5060 Ti x2 pair (2026-09-28, healthy state)

`results/session2/pair-5060ti-x2/smoke.py`, swift-daily flags (ctx 90112, MTP 3/0/0, ub 128),
both cards 150 W, cold prompts, 512 generated tokens, one sample per depth.

| Depth | pp 71,29 / 50,50 | tg 71,29 / 50,50 | MTP accepted 71,29 / 50,50 | min free GPU0/GPU1 MiB 71,29 / 50,50 |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 512 | 482 / 492 | 56.1 / 75.8 | 330/542 / 330/542 | 1999/9211 / 5286/5885 |
| 8k | 828 / 864 | 59.6 / 80.0 | 340/513 / 343/504 | 2011/9209 / 5284/5883 |
| 32k | 803 / 822 | 53.4 / 48.0 | 337/521 / 271/715 | 1997/9209 / 5264/5883 |
| 64k | 723 / 754 | 36.9 / 44.7 | 317/580 / 304/618 | 1916/9209 / 5263/5883 |
| 86k | 664 / 717 | 48.1 / 58.5 | 363/442 / 360/452 | 1866/9209 / 5277/5883 |

Where MTP acceptance matches (512, 8k, 86k), 50,50 decodes 22-35% faster. Deep single samples
are dominated by acceptance variance (32k, 64k), so they need repeats. Prefill +2-8%. 50,50 also
leaves ~5.3 GB free on the display GPU against ~1.9 GB. Peak 70 C.

### The modprobe hook did not run at boot (11:06 boot), and a hard freeze (2026-09-28)

After the reboot, `LnkCtl2: Target Link Speed: 16GT/s` on 00:03.2: the cap was not applied. Cause:
`nvidia` loads from the **initramfs** (8 `nvidia*.ko` in `/boot/initrd.img-$(uname -r)`, NVRM at ~4 s),
whose copy of `modprobe.d` predates the hook and which has no `setpci`. The 11:06 boot froze hard at
the start of a tensor `llama-bench` (180 W limits, card 2 uncapped); no Xid/AER/watchdog was logged,
last journal line 11:08:30. Uncapped card 2 is the leading suspect; 180 W is the second.

Recovered with cap + sysfs remove/rescan; `LnkCtl2` then stayed at 8GT/s after the driver restarted
card 2, so the driver does not reset the target itself.

Fix, applied 2026-09-28 and verified after the 11:15 reboot (`LnkCtl2` 8GT/s, no Xid): an initramfs-tools hook that adds `setpci`, then rebuild (Pop!_OS
`zz-kernelstub` copies the new initrd to the EFI partition):

    sudo install -m 755 <scratchpad>/initramfs-hook-pcie-gen3-cap /etc/initramfs-tools/hooks/pcie-gen3-cap
    sudo update-initramfs -u
    lsinitramfs /boot/initrd.img-$(uname -r) | grep -E 'bin/setpci|pcie-gen3-cap.conf'

Then reboot and confirm `sudo lspci -s 00:03.2 -vv | grep LnkCtl2` shows 8GT/s before any GPU load.
Until then, every boot needs the manual cap + remove/rescan. Also set 150 W at every boot.

## Eight-task Swift long tail on the 5060 Ti x2 pair (2026-09-28)

Runner `results/session2/pair-5060ti-x2/run_swift_longtail.py`, which calls `swift_longtail.main`
unchanged except second device, split `50,50`, and caps 150/150 W. Campaign
`results/session2/pair-5060ti-x2/campaign-swift-iq4-xs-longtail-20260928`. Pinned binary 03fa73cb2,
ctx 102400, ub 128, MTP 3/0/0, seed 1729. No Xid during the run.

| | 5060 Ti + 2060S, 69,31 (20260914-rerun2) | 5060 Ti x2, 50,50 | Change |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Solved | 8/8 | 8/8 | |
| Wall | 2349.7 s | 1640.2 s | -30.2% |
| Output tokens | 106,574 | 102,748 | -3.6% |
| Decode time | 2022.0 s | 1376.9 s | -31.9% |
| Decode rate (tokens / decode s) | 52.7 tok/s | 74.6 tok/s | +41.6% |
| Prefill time | 231.8 s | 175.1 s | -24.5% |
| MTP acceptance | 67.3% | 67.5% | |
| Peak context | 36,145 | 37,941 | |

One run per arm. Acceptance and output volume match, so the decode-rate gain is not an MTP or
output-length artifact. Run-to-run spread of this suite is not measured for this pair.

## NCCL vs internal AllReduce on the 5060 Ti x2 pair (2026-09-28)

Same binary for both arms: `.cache/llama-nccl-03fa73cb2` (pinned source 03fa73cb2, built 09-12 with NCCL,
now loading system `libnccl.so.2` 2.32.3+cuda13.4 without a rebuild). The only change is
`GGML_CUDA_ALLREDUCE=internal|nccl`. NCCL transport is `SHM/direct` both ways (no P2P, as expected on GeForce).
Raw output: `results/session2/pair-5060ti-x2/nccl/`.

`llama-bench -sm tensor -ts 50/50 -ub 128 -r 3`, two interleaved rounds (sd < 0.2% in every cell):

| | internal | nccl |
|---|---:|---:|
| pp2048 | 945.2 / 944.0 | 1017.1 / 1021.1 |
| tg64 | 44.33 / 44.28 | 40.61 / 40.59 |

`smoke.py` at 50,50, MTP on, one pass per arm (pp / tg tok/s, draft accepted/drafted):

| Depth | internal | nccl |
|---:|---|---|
| 512 | 489 / 75.5 (330/542) | 507 / 65.1 (317/579) |
| 8192 | 863 / 79.9 (343/504) | 918 / 74.1 (345/496) |
| 32768 | 823 / 49.1 (271/715) | 875 / 60.6 (332/537) |
| 65536 | 757 / 47.8 (304/618) | 799 / 49.9 (325/556) |
| 86000 | 717 / 58.8 (360/452) | 758 / 54.4 (357/458) |

The internal arm reproduces the pinned `build/` smoke to within 0.5 tok/s with identical acceptance.
Where acceptance matches (512, 8k, 86k), NCCL decodes 7-14% slower. At 32k and 64k, NCCL produced
different tokens with higher acceptance, so those rows do not compare decode speed. NCCL prefill is 5-8% faster.

Decision: keep the internal AllReduce. The eight-task run spent 1377 s in decode and 175 s in
prefill, so +6% prefill cannot offset -8% decode. This agrees with `pair-5060ti-2060s/NCCL.md`.

## Upstream llama.cpp ed7ac35e1 (2026-09-28): no regression; the first build was misconfigured

**Correction.** The regression below was a build-environment fault, not an upstream change. `nvcc` on `PATH`
is the distro CUDA 12.0 (`/usr/bin/nvcc`), so a fresh configure with only `CMAKE_CUDA_COMPILER` set made
`FindCUDAToolkit` link `/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libcudart.so.12` and `libcublas.so.12`. Rebuilding the pinned
03fa73cb2 that way reproduced the same slowdown (pp2048 778, 32k server prefill 359). Fix: configure with
`-DCUDAToolkit_ROOT=/usr/local/cuda-13.2` and check `ldd libggml-cuda.so` shows `libcublas.so.13` from
`/usr/local/cuda`. Correct builds (`~/repos/llama.cpp-bisect`, NCCL off, internal AllReduce):

| | pp2048 | tg64 | smoke 512 pp / tg | smoke 32k pp / tg | draft 32k |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|---|
| 03fa73cb2 rebuilt | 940.0 | 44.17 | 452 / 75.7 | 816 / 48.1 | 271/715 |
| ed7ac35e1 | 947.6 | 44.24 | 458 / 75.8 | 817 / 48.9 | 271/715 |

Upstream matches pinned, with identical draft acceptance. There is no gain and no loss, so there is no reason to switch. The CUDA 12
builds, the commit ranking, and the three bisection steps built on them were void and are deleted.

For new builds, use the local preset `~/repos/llama.cpp/CMakeUserPresets.json` (git-excluded through
`.git/info/exclude`): `cmake --preset cuda13 && cmake --build --preset cuda13` builds into `build-cuda13/`
with CUDA 13.2, archs `75;120`, and NCCL off. The pinned `build/` is untouched.

### Original (invalid) measurement

Worktree `~/repos/llama.cpp-ed7ac35e1`, 366 commits after the pinned 03fa73cb2. Built with the pinned
settings (CUDA 13.2, archs `75;120`, native, Release). All benchmark flags still exist.

**Trap:** `GGML_CUDA_NCCL` defaults to ON, and `libnccl-dev` is now installed, so any fresh build links
NCCL. With NCCL linked, the Linux default is `GGML_CUDA_ALLREDUCE=nccl` (`ggml-cuda.cu`
`ggml_backend_cuda_comm_init`), which decodes about 8% slower on this pair. Set
`GGML_CUDA_ALLREDUCE=internal`, or configure with `-DGGML_CUDA_NCCL=OFF`, for every new build.

`llama-bench -sm tensor -ts 50/50 -ub 128 -r 3`, two interleaved rounds:

| | pinned, internal | upstream, internal | upstream, nccl |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| pp2048 | 945.7 / 941.8 | 780.5 / 783.6 | 835.5 / 835.1 |
| tg64 | 44.33 / 44.27 | 44.45 / 44.52 | 40.73 / 40.72 |

`smoke.py` at 50,50 with MTP, internal AllReduce (pp / tg tok/s, draft accepted/drafted):

| Depth | pinned | upstream |
|---:|---|---|
| 512 | 492 / 75.8 (330/542) | 114 / 75.8 (336/525) |
| 8192 | 864 / 80.0 (343/504) | 594 / 71.5 (348/489) |
| 32768 | 822 / 48.0 (271/715) | 350 / 42.0 (322/567) |
| 65536 | 754 / 44.7 (304/618) | 224 / 33.7 (347/492) |
| 86000 | 717 / 58.5 (360/452) | 183 / 31.9 (365/438) |

Upstream prefill is 17% slower in `llama-bench` (no MTP) and 30-75% slower in the server with MTP.
The loss grows with depth. Upstream decode matches pinned without MTP, but in the server it is 11-45%
slower at 8k and deeper, with equal or better acceptance. The server log shows no new warning.

## Speed screening on Swift 1.0 IQ4_XS, 5060 Ti x2 (2026-09-28)

These are smoke screens (`smoke.py`, repo-text continuation, one prompt per depth, `ignore_eos`). Acceptance on this
text is not representative of agentic tasks, and one prompt can swing 32k acceptance from 38% to 64%. Only the
eight-task run decides. Tok/s is decode at depths 512 / 8k / 32k. Raw output: `results/session2/pair-5060ti-x2/`
`draft-sweep/`, `dflash/`, `pr29187/`.

| Config | tg 512 / 8k / 32k | accepted/drafted at 8k |
|---|---|---|
| MTP n-max 2 | 71.9 / 73.3 / 65.3 | 302/418 |
| MTP n-max 3 (current) | 75.3 / 79.9 / 48.5 | 343/504 |
| MTP n-max 4 | 75.3 / 84.3 / 74.3 | 368/570 |
| MTP n-max 5 | 71.2 / 69.6 / 71.4 | 350/804 |
| DFlash2 Q8_0 n-max 7 (upstream + PR #27858) | 54.9 / 67.0 / 55.1 | 378/926 |
| DFlash2 Q8_0 n-max 3 | 64.1 / 71.7 / 43.6 | 347/489 |
| PR #29187 GDN fusion, MTP n-max 3 | 77.2 / 79.2 / 66.7 | 343/501 |

- **DFlash:** `z-lab/Qwen3.8-27B-DFlash2-GGUF` asserts under `-sm tensor` on pinned and upstream
  (`ggml-backend-meta.cpp:543`, issue #27819). PR #27858 fixes it. The drafter is trained on base Qwen3.8, and
  its acceptance on Swift is low (31-41% at n-max 7), so it loses to MTP. Retest if a Swift-trained DFlash GGUF appears.
- **PR #29187:** llama-bench tg64 44.57 vs 44.26 upstream (+0.6%), pp unchanged, `test-backend-ops -o GATED_DELTA_NET`
  passes. The gain is too small to carry the patch. Launch count is not the decode bottleneck on this pair.
- **MTP n-max 4, eight-task run** (`campaign-swift-iq4-xs-longtail-20260928-nmax4`, pinned build): 8/8 solved, wall 1439 s
  vs 1640 s, output 92,426 vs 102,748 tokens, decode 77.7 vs 74.6 tok/s (+4%), acceptance 60.6% vs 67.5%.
  Most of the wall-time difference is 10% fewer output tokens, which is run variation. The +4% decode is under the
  10% bar, so n-max 3 stays.
- **Swift 1.5 IQ4_XS** (`.cache/hillclimb-models/swift15-iq4-xs/`) carries the MTP head (`blk.64.nextn.*`).
  Downloaded, not yet run.

## NVFP4 and quant quality research (2026-09-28, web research, not measured here)

- Swift 1.5 GGUF card (`ukisai/Swift-1.5-Qwen3.8-27B-GGUF`), KLD vs BF16 at 32k / top-token agreement:
  IQ4_XS 0.0173 / 94.96%, Q4_K_M 0.0134 / 95.0%, Q5_K_M 0.0061 / 96.9%, Q6_K 0.0016 / 98.3%.
  UkisAI recommends Q6_K or higher for long agentic runs.
- NVFP4 trails 4-bit GGUF on quality. `akopytko/Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4-GGUF` (base Qwen3.8-27B, one llama.cpp harness):
  NVFP4 KLD 0.066-0.071 vs Q4_0 0.028-0.038 vs Q6_K 0.003. Decode is the same as Q4_0, prefill is +50%.
  `nvidia/Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4` loses about 1-1.5 pt on GPQA and Terminal-Bench. No Swift NVFP4 quality data exists.
- UkisAI NVFP4 layout (ModelOpt mixed): NVFP4 MLP + lm_head, FP8 GDN/attention projections, BF16 MTP head, 21.9 GB.
- Speed on 2x 5060 Ti (published): ninfer TP2 fork `lynx-gt/ninfer-tp2-5060ti` gives 76-86 tok/s with the official
  mixed checkpoint + MTP3, and 107-126 only with W4A4 checkpoints that are not Swift. vLLM 0.30 TP2 gives 78 tok/s
  prose / 125 structured. Our llama.cpp IQ4_XS + MTP gives about 75. Decode gain is small, and the quality cost is real.
- Decision: do not pursue NVFP4 for quality. Swift 1.5 Q6_K (23.9 GB) should fit the pair at about 90k context.
  A ninfer TP2 trial (`CaptainArni/Swift-1.5-Qwen3.8-27B-NInfer`) is optional, for prefill and tool-output speed only.

Models moved on 2026-09-28: every GGUF from `.cache/hillclimb-models/<id>/` now lives in `~/models/` under its original
file name, with a symlink at the old path, so campaign pins and scripts keep working. `serve.sh` uses `~/models` directly.

## W4A16 (AutoRound/AWQ/GPTQ) research (2026-09-28, web research, not measured here)

- Swift 1.5 W4A16 files are about 19.5 GB, but the int4 body is only about 12.3 GB (4.1-4.25 bpw, the IQ4_XS class).
  The rest is BF16 embed (2.5 GB), BF16 lm_head (2.5 GB), and the BF16 MTP head (0.85 GB). No KLD is published for any W4A16
  Swift or Qwen3.8 export. Cross-engine task scores put W4A16 AutoRound level with Unsloth IQ4_XS (86.0% vs 86.9%).
  It is not Q5/Q6 class.
- MTP acceptance does not depend on format. ninfer's controlled Qwen3.8 table: int 58.5% vs nvfp4 60.8%. The earlier "68% vs 46%"
  compared different models. Swift 1.5 GPTQ with a BF16 head measured 68% (Arc B70), which matches our 67.5%.
  Head precision barely matters (bartowski #4: Q4_0 head is 64.1% vs F16 66.0%, and Q4_0 is faster end to end).
- Speed: stock vLLM W4A16 is expected at about 55-70 tok/s here (BF16 lm_head read on every draft/verify step).
  The ~98-100 tok/s single-stream results on 2x 5060 Ti come from the HyperQwen vLLM patch stack
  (https://github.com/syv-ai/HyperQwen: int4 lm_head, pruned Swift draft vocab PR #139, int8 KV on TRITON_ATTN).
  They were measured on short prompts only. One run used P2P, one used `NCCL_P2P_DISABLE=1` with DFlash2.
- Known vLLM risks: MTP `fc` load crash for WNA16 checkpoints with a BF16 head (vllm#53387, fixes unmerged), and NaN
  after prefix-cache hits with mamba align (#55766).
- Decision: stock W4A16 is not worth it. HyperQwen is the only engine-side candidate worth a trial, and it must be
  judged on the eight-task run at 32k+ context.

## Upstream ed7ac35e1 eight-task run (2026-09-28)

`campaign-swift-iq4-xs-longtail-20260928-upstream` (upstream `build/`, CUDA 13.2, NCCL off, MTP n-max 3): 8/8 solved,
wall 2005 s, output 124,438 tokens, decode 1709 s = 72.8 tok/s (pinned: 74.6), acceptance 67.4% (pinned: 67.5%),
peak context 45,860 (pinned: 37,941). Speed and acceptance match pinned within run variation. The model wrote 21% more
tokens, which is sampling variation at temp 1: a seed does not fix the path, because GPU rounding and MTP batch shapes
differ between runs. Per-task wall time from single runs is mostly noise; compare decode tok/s and solve counts.
Decision: upstream is the engine for the Swift 1.5 runs. Switching `serve.sh` waits for Igor.

## Q5_K_M speed check and HyperQwen first light (2026-09-28)

Raw decode (upstream llama-bench tg64, no MTP), Swift 1.5: tensor IQ4_XS 43.3 / Q5_K_M 34.5 / Q6_K 30.9; layer 25.9 / 19.6 / 17.4.
Decode scales with file size (Q5 is 12% faster than Q6). With MTP the fixed per-step costs shrink the gaps (IQ4 vs Q6 1.40x raw,
1.21x with MTP). The Q5≈Q6 smoke tie was single-prompt acceptance noise. No alignment anomaly.

HyperQwen (https://github.com/syv-ai/HyperQwen, `~/repos/HyperQwen`, native venv, vLLM 0.30.0 + its patch series, `verify.sh` OK).
Model: `liamwh/Swift-Qwen3.8-27B-W4A16-syv-fast` (Swift 1.0 AWQ body, int4 lm_head/MTP, Swift draft vocab), `~/models/hyperqwen/`.
Launch (needs `GPU_UTIL=0.88`; 0.93 OOMs because of the desktop on card 0):

    cd ~/repos/HyperQwen && env CUDA_DEVICE_ORDER=PCI_BUS_ID CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0,1 NCCL_P2P_DISABLE=1 NCCL_CUMEM_ENABLE=0 \
      HOST=127.0.0.1 PORT=18020 GPU_UTIL=0.88 MODEL=$HOME/models/hyperqwen/Swift-Qwen3.8-27B-W4A16-syv-fast \
      SPEC=mtp CTX=long MAX_LEN=102400 MAX_SEQS=1 EXTRA_ARGS="--tensor-parallel-size 2 --disable-custom-all-reduce" \
      bash single-user/start_qwen.sh

Starts in about 225 s (compile + graphs). KV pool 300k tokens (fp8 KV). `smoke_vllm.py` (same corpus/depths/sampling as smoke.py,
except no min_p, which vLLM rejects with speculative decoding):

| Depth | HyperQwen pp / tg (accepted/drafted) | llama.cpp Swift 1.0 IQ4_XS pp / tg |
|---:|---|---|
| 512 | 972 / 120.3 (352/477) | 492 / 75.8 |
| 8192 | 997 / 98.2 (318/582) | 864 / 80.0 |
| 32768 | 1156 / 109.4 (346/504) | 822 / 48.0 (unlucky acceptance) |
| 90000 | 1151 / 101.7 (346/495) | 717 / 58.5 (at 86000) |

A screen, not a verdict: an eight-task run needs the agentic harness pointed at the vLLM endpoint.

## HyperQwen eight-task adapter (2026-09-28)

Same assets, Codex config, jail, grader and `agentic_probe.probe` as the llama long tail; the probe takes an
`engine=` hook (default `None` = the llama-server contract, unchanged). One long-lived server, benchmark configuration:

    cd ~/repos/HyperQwen && setsid nohup env CUDA_DEVICE_ORDER=PCI_BUS_ID CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0,1 NCCL_P2P_DISABLE=1 \
      NCCL_CUMEM_ENABLE=0 VLLM_SERVER_DEV_MODE=1 HOST=127.0.0.1 PORT=8081 GPU_UTIL=0.88 \
      MODEL=$HOME/models/hyperqwen/Swift-Qwen3.8-27B-W4A16-syv-fast SPEC=mtp CTX=long MAX_LEN=102400 MAX_SEQS=1 PREFIX_CACHE=1 \
      EXTRA_ARGS="--tensor-parallel-size 2 --disable-custom-all-reduce --served-model-name local-benchmark --chat-template $HOME/repos/qwen38-eval/results/session2/pair-5060ti-x2/hyperqwen/chat-template-bartowski-leading-instructions-v1.jinja --override-generation-config {\"temperature\":1.0,\"top_k\":20,\"top_p\":0.95}" \
      bash single-user/start_qwen.sh > serve-bench.log 2>&1 < /dev/null & echo $! > ~/repos/HyperQwen/serve.pid

Eight tasks (resumable; `--task X` repeatable for a subset, into its own campaign dir):

    cd ~/repos/qwen38-eval && PYTHONPATH=. python3 results/session2/pair-5060ti-x2/run_vllm_longtail.py \
      --campaign results/session2/pair-5060ti-x2/campaign-hyperqwen-longtail-20260928 \
      --assets results/session2/pair-5060ti-2060s/campaign-confirmed-fullsuite-20260914-r8/snapshot/agentic-assets

Differences from the llama path: no min_p (vLLM refuses it with speculative decoding; llama used 0.05). Prompt reuse is vLLM
prefix caching (block-aligned on this hybrid model, about 78-92% of prompt tokens cached against llama's ~91%), reset through
`/reset_prefix_cache` before each task, so each task starts cold as on llama's fresh server. The context peak is the largest
input+output of one `/v1/responses` call, read by a relay on the inference bridge (`model-calls.jsonl` per attempt), not
`n_tokens_max`. The chat template is byte-identical (sha256 4af3dcf0...). Reasoning is parsed by vLLM's `qwen3` parser and
replayed in history, like llama's `--reasoning-preserve` default.

Validation, one run each: `javascript/affine-cipher` solved, 50.9 s wall, 2,545 output tokens, 115.9 tok/s decode,
MTP 71.9%, peak 14,778. `rust/book-store` solved, 319.1 s, 32,058 output tokens (llama run: 12,935), 114.8 tok/s decode
(llama: 76.5), MTP 72.5%, peak 45,372. No Xid.

## HyperQwen eight-task run (2026-09-28)

`campaign-hyperqwen-longtail-20260928` (HyperQwen vLLM 0.30, Swift 1.0 W4A16 syv-fast, TP2 without P2P, MTP k=3, fp8 KV,
prefix cache on and reset per task, no min_p): 8/8 solved, wall 1872 s, output 143,602 tokens, prefill 405 s, decode 1311 s
= 109.6 tok/s, acceptance 70.2%, peak context 67,450.

| | llama.cpp Swift 1.0 IQ4_XS (pinned / upstream) | HyperQwen Swift 1.0 W4A16 |
|---|---|---:|
| Decode | 74.6 / 72.8 tok/s | 109.6 tok/s (+47%) |
| Output tokens | 102.7k / 124.4k | 143.6k |
| Prefill time | 175 / 203 s | 405 s |
| Wall | 1640 / 2005 s | 1872 s |
| Wall per output token | 16.0 / 16.1 ms | 13.0 ms (-19%) |
| Acceptance | 67.5% / 67.4% | 70.2% |

Decode is about 1.47x faster on real agentic turns. Two costs eat part of it: (1) prefill is about 2x, because vLLM's prefix cache
matches only at block boundaries on this hybrid model (78-92% reuse vs about 91% on llama), and (2) the model wrote more
tokens. Whether that comes from sampling without min_p or from run variation is not resolved by one run. Both engines think
at reasoning effort xhigh (Swift template default; llama.cpp forwards `reasoning.effort` too).

## Swift 1.5 HyperQwen checkpoint (2026-09-28, CPU only, not booted)

`~/models/hyperqwen/Swift-1.5-Qwen3.8-27B-W4A16-syv-fast`, from
[`ukisai/Swift-1.5-Qwen3.8-27b-INT4@81a28ee7`](https://huggingface.co/ukisai/Swift-1.5-Qwen3.8-27b-INT4/tree/81a28ee75eb1cd469f209813a368a79a7e3db3bc)
(llm-compressor AWQ smoothing + GPTQ, W4A16 g128 **symmetric**, compressed-tensors, FP8 KV scales for the 16
full-attention layers, BF16 lm_head / embed / MTP). Why this source: it is the only one of the three candidates in
compressed-tensors, the format the `prepare/` scripts and the Marlin path take. The AutoRound export is
`packing_format: auto_round:auto_gptq`, and bjonor's is native GPTQ. Both would need a repack first (the ThinkingCap case in
`docs/third-party-checkpoints.md`). Quantizing `mtp.fc` also avoids vllm#53387, the crash on a BF16 `mtp.fc` with a WNA16 config.
The closest match to the Swift 1.0 recipe is `ukisai/Swift-1.5-Qwen3.8-27b-W4A16-AWQ` (asymmetric AWQ, like TheUnderscore's).
It was not chosen because its GatedDeltaNet projections are RTN, not calibrated.

Steps (HyperQwen venv, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=` throughout):

1. `hf download ... --revision 81a28ee7 --exclude '*.mp4' --exclude '*.png'`: 19.4 GB.
2. `prepare/quant_heads_stream.py <dir>`: lm_head and embed_tokens to int8 g128 (rel err 0.0069 / 0.0065), MTP to int8
   g128 including `fc` (0.0066-0.0154). Took 2 min, 5.9 GB RSS.
3. Removed `re:^mtp.*` from `quantization_config.ignore`. `quant_heads_stream.py` drops only the exact `lm_head` and
   MTP-linear names, so with the regex left in, vLLM would ignore `group_3` and look for a BF16 `mtp.fc.weight`. Confirmed on the CPU with
   vLLM's `find_matched_target` / `should_ignore_layer`: mtp.* goes to group_3, lm_head and mtp.draft_lm_head to group_1,
   embed to group_2 (all int8), and the body to group_0 int4.
4. `prepare/build_draft_vocab.py <dir> --ids swift_draft_vocab_ids.json`: the **Swift 1.0 list** (25,879 ids, reused
   because counting Swift 1.5's own outputs needs the model running, which means a GPU). The tokenizer vocab, merges and added tokens
   are identical to Swift 1.0, and `mtp_draft_vocab_ids.pt` matches Swift 1.0's exactly. Draft head: 135 MB int8.
5. Deleted the `*.bak-orig` BF16 shards (7.5 GB, disk at 99%). A later GPU GPTQ head calibration has to download
   `model-00001`, `model-00005` and `model_mtp.safetensors` again at the same revision.

Differences from the Swift 1.0 syv-fast recipe: the lm_head and MTP are **int8 RTN**, not int4 GPTQ calibrated on the finetune's
hidden states, because that capture needs a GPU. int8 RTN is higher fidelity than int4 GPTQ but reads more: weights total 16.7 GB
against 15.5 GB (+0.6 GB lm_head, +0.2 GB MTP, +0.07 GB draft head). That is about +0.55 GB per card at TP2, so the
`GPU_UTIL=0.88 MAX_LEN=102400` pool shrinks. The body is symmetric GPTQ, not asymmetric AWQ. `CTX=long` uses the checkpoint's
calibrated FP8 KV scales (Swift 1.0 had none). `patches/triton-spec-attn-fp8-kv.patch` honours `_k_scale` / `_v_scale`.

`MODEL=<dir> CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES= bash verify.sh --no-server`: all 8 model checks PASS (symmetric zero points on 411
packed modules, int8 lm_head geometry, embed, MTP, draft head, 7 shards, no cross-shard duplicates, tokenizer). The one
FAIL is `torch cannot see a CUDA GPU`, caused by hiding the GPUs. The Swift 1.0 dir gives the same result under the same command.
The WARNs are environment-only: KVarN, no AutoRound-fast, no DFlash2, no API key, unit inactive. Not yet booted.

Launch: the benchmark command from "HyperQwen eight-task adapter" with only `MODEL=` changed to
`$HOME/models/hyperqwen/Swift-1.5-Qwen3.8-27B-W4A16-syv-fast`.

## vLLM investigation, u64 run, and min_p (2026-09-28)

vLLM investigation (`results/session2/pair-5060ti-x2/vllm-investigation/`): the NaN-after-prefix-hit bug (vllm#55766, open) and the
concurrent spec-decode crash (race fixed by open vllm#53613) did NOT reproduce here (80 targeted probes, 96 replayed turns, 4
concurrent agents over 101k tokens). MTP keeps the last prefill chunk >= 832 tokens, which avoids #55766's trigger. The prefix-cache
unit is 832 tokens, and MTP drops one more block per turn. `--prefix-match-unit 64` (already in 0.30.0) cut recomputed tokens 40% on replays.

u64 eight-task run (`campaign-hyperqwen-u64-longtail-20260928`, Swift 1.0 W4A16, otherwise identical): 8/8 solved, wall 2580 s,
output 217,645 tokens (vs 143,602), decode 104.5 tok/s, acceptance 69.4%, prefill 380 s (vs 405 s) despite more turns, peak context
87,958. One call ran away to 72,196 output tokens from a 15.7k prompt. Token volume swamps the prefill comparison. The replay
measurement (-40% recompute) is the cleaner number for u64.

min_p: vLLM 0.30's V2 model runner already applies min_p to the target logits in spec-decode verification; only the validator in
`sampling_params.py:_validate_spec_decode` refused it, and the Responses API dropped it. Fixed locally by
`~/repos/HyperQwen/patches/minp-spec-decode-v2-responses.patch` (added to `patches/series`). Launch with `VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER=1`
and `--override-generation-config` including `"min_p":0.05,"max_new_tokens":32768` (per-call runaway cap).

## HyperQwen + min_p eight-task run (2026-09-28)

`campaign-hyperqwen-u64-minp-longtail-20260928` (Swift 1.0 W4A16, u64, min_p 0.05 via the local patch, V2 runner, 32k per-call cap,
GPU_UTIL 0.86 because 0.88 OOMed on the first min_p request): 8/8 solved, wall 1396 s, output 107,038 tokens, prefill 208 s,
decode 1095 s = 97.8 tok/s, acceptance 68.7%, peak context 45,242. No runaway calls, no Xid.

| | llama.cpp pinned IQ4_XS | HyperQwen no min_p | HyperQwen + min_p |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Wall | 1640 s | 1872 s | **1396 s (-15% vs llama)** |
| Output tokens | 102.7k | 143.6k | 107.0k |
| Prefill | 175 s | 405 s | 208 s |
| Decode | 74.6 tok/s | 109.6 tok/s | 97.8 tok/s |
| Acceptance | 67.5% | 70.2% | 68.7% |

min_p removed the token inflation: output is back to llama.cpp's level, so the excess came from the sampling tail, not quant damage
(one run). Decode fell from 109.6 to 97.8 tok/s. Candidates: min_p's extra sampler work per draft position, u64, or task mix (one
run). Prefill is 208 s vs 405 s: fewer turns plus u64 reuse. Best configuration so far.

## HyperQwen + min_p, default prefix unit (2026-09-28)

`campaign-hyperqwen-minp-longtail-20260928` (as the u64+min_p run but prefix unit 832): 8/8 solved, wall 1371 s, output 102,880,
prefill 281 s, decode 101.5 tok/s, acceptance 71.6%, peak 45,724.

| | llama.cpp pinned | HQ no min_p | HQ u64 no min_p | HQ min_p (832) | HQ min_p + u64 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Wall | 1640 s | 1872 s | 2580 s | 1371 s | 1396 s |
| Output tokens | 102.7k | 143.6k | 217.6k | 102.9k | 107.0k |
| Prefill | 175 s | 405 s | 380 s | 281 s | 208 s |
| Decode | 74.6 | 109.6 | 104.5 | 101.5 | 97.8 tok/s |
| Acceptance | 67.5% | 70.2% | 69.4% | 71.6% | 68.7% |

Both min_p runs write llama.cpp's token volume, which confirms min_p fixes the inflation. u64 cuts prefill by 26% (281 -> 208 s);
the two min_p runs finish within 2% wall of each other, about 16% faster than llama.cpp pinned. Decode with min_p sits
around 98-102 tok/s (vs 105-110 without); the min_p sampler costs a few percent. u64's decode effect is inside the noise.
Recommended HyperQwen config: min_p 0.05 + u64.

## Swift 1.5 on HyperQwen: chunk4096, k=4 attempt, and lost-task repeats (2026-09-29 overnight)

- `campaign-hyperqwen15-u64-minp-chunk4096-longtail-20260928` (`--max-num-batched-tokens 4096`): 8/8, 1599 s, 120.7k output tokens,
  prefill 230 s (base 2048: 230 s, so no gain), 95.5 tok/s, accept 71.7%.
- MTP k=4 did not start: with k=4 the KV block is 848 tokens and `--prefix-match-unit 64` must divide it. Retry queued with unit 16.
- Lost-task repeats (book-store, complex-numbers, react, forth), output tokens, all runs 4/4 solved:

| Run | book-store | complex-numbers | react | forth | sum |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| HQ 1.0 u64+min_p | 10.2k | 11.0k | 14.7k | 13.7k | 49.5k |
| HQ 1.0 min_p u832 | 12.8k | 7.7k | 12.7k | 9.2k | 42.3k |
| HQ 1.5 base (8-task run) | 25.7k | 22.1k | 22.6k | 18.4k | 88.8k |
| HQ 1.5 chunk4096 (8-task run) | 25.1k | 11.0k | 18.4k | 14.2k | 68.6k |
| HQ 1.5 rep1 | 16.8k | 2.0k | 19.2k | 18.9k | 56.9k |
| HQ 1.5 rep2 | 20.6k | 4.8k | 13.5k | 13.3k | 52.3k |
| HQ 1.5 rep3 | 8.0k | 3.1k | 39.0k | 15.5k | 65.5k |
| llama.cpp 1.5 IQ4_XS | 9.9k | 3.4k | 23.3k | 12.5k | 49.1k |
| llama.cpp 1.5 Q6_K | 10.3k | 2.9k | 17.0k | 12.2k | 42.5k |

Reading: the first HQ 1.5 run (88.8k) was a high draw, but not all noise. The three fresh repeats average 58.2k, against 45.9k for
HQ 1.0 (2 runs) and 45.8k for Swift 1.5 on llama.cpp (2 runs): about +27%. All 5 HQ 1.5 samples sit above all 4 other samples.
The excess is concentrated in book-store (8–26k on HQ 1.5 vs 10–13k elsewhere) and react. complex-numbers returned to normal
(the 22k jest rabbit hole was luck). Suspects, in order: calibrated FP8 KV scales (1.5-only; saturate on agentic text; kvs1 test
queued), then the UkisAI GPTQ int4 body. Sample sizes are small (2 runs for the controls); treat +27% as likely-real but unconfirmed.

## llama.cpp MTP cost per draft step (2026-09-29 01:32)

`smoke.py`, pinned build, Swift 1.0 IQ4_XS, split 50,50, 512 generated tokens, one pass per arm (`smoke/mtp-cost-*.json`).
Steps = generated − accepted; step time = decode ms / steps.

| Draft max k | tok/step 512 / 8k | Step ms 512 / 8k | Added vs k−1 (ms) |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 0 (MTP off) | 1.00 / 1.00 | 23.0 / 23.7 | — |
| 1 | 1.80 / 1.83 | 29.3 / 30.0 | +6.2 / +6.3 |
| 2 | 2.36 / 2.44 | 33.0 / 34.0 | +3.7 / +3.9 |
| 3 | 2.81 / 3.03 | 37.7 / 38.2 | +4.8 / +4.2 |

Each extra draft step costs ~4 ms; turning speculation on costs ~6.3 ms (one draft step plus the wider verify and its bookkeeping).
Streaming the full-vocab Q6_K lm_head per draft step is ~1.2 ms of that (0.5 GB/card at 432 GB/s) and the MTP layer ~0.25 ms, so
~2.5–3 ms per draft step is not bandwidth: most likely per-step host round-trips (separate graph evaluation + CPU sampling).
HyperQwen does a whole k=3 step in 31.3 ms vs 38 ms here at the same ~3.0 tok/step. The draft-vocab trim (PR #29143) would recover
~1 ms/draft step (~3 ms/step, ~8%); removing the host round-trip per draft step is the bigger lever. Single pass each; ±5% noise.

## Swift 1.5 IQ4_XS vs Q6_K on the four lost tasks (llama.cpp upstream, 2026-09-29 01:32–04:18)

Same engine, prompt and flags; only the quant differs; runs interleaved IQ4/Q6. Q6_K rep1 was lost: the campaign stopped itself
with `system_ram_below_2gib` 34 min into book-store (likely cause, unproven: the user's other sessions ran `forge`/`solc` builds
concurrently). The 8-task run of each quant contributes one more sample. Output tokens, four-task sum (all runs 4/4 solved):

| Config | n | Sums | Median | Per-task median (book / complex / react / forth) |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| llama.cpp Swift 1.5 IQ4_XS | 4 | 49.1k, 44.5k, 62.3k, 51.8k | 50.4k | 11.7k / 6.1k / 12.7k / 15.3k |
| llama.cpp Swift 1.5 Q6_K | 3 | 42.5k, 111.6k, 72.9k | 72.9k | 13.2k / 18.4k / 22.9k / 12.2k |
| HyperQwen Swift 1.5 W4A16 | 5 | 88.8k, 68.6k, 56.9k, 52.3k, 65.5k | 65.5k | 20.6k / 4.8k / 19.2k / 15.5k |
| HyperQwen Swift 1.0 W4A16 | 2 | 49.5k, 42.3k | 45.9k | 11.5k / 9.3k / 13.7k / 11.4k |

Reading: higher precision does NOT make Swift 1.5 terser on these tasks — Q6_K is, if anything, wordier (one 111.6k run with the
complex-numbers jest rabbit hole at 38k and react at 43k). So the "Q6 picks better paths" hypothesis is not supported, and token
volume is a poor proxy for quant quality at these sample sizes (per-config spread ~1.5–2.6×). Fidelity will come from the KLD pass.
HQ 1.5's excess vs HQ 1.0 and llama IQ4_XS remains (median 65.5k vs 45.9k / 50.4k) but is within the spread Q6_K shows; the
kvs1 run tests the one HQ-1.5-specific mechanism.

## Xid 79 on card 1 during step-0 profiling (2026-09-29 04:51:13) — queue stopped, reboot required

- `NVRM: Xid (PCI:0000:08:00): 79, pid=…, name=VLLM::Worker, GPU has fallen off the bus`, then Xid 154 on both GPUs:
  "GPU recovery action … Node Reboot Required". The GPU that fell off is **card 1: 08:00 on root port 00:03.1, the direct slot at
  Gen4 16 GT/s**, NOT the Gen3-riser card (09:00 on 00:03.2, which the modprobe cap covers). It also drives the desktop, so the
  compositor now logs nvidia-drm alloc errors.
- Moment: during the boot of a HyperQwen server (step-0 A/B arm `ab-proto-LL-2`, `NCCL_PROTO=LL`); the previous LL boot (LL-1)
  was fine. No NVRM/AER/PCIe message in the kernel log between 04:30 and the Xid. Power limits 150 W; temps were normal all night.
  Uptime before the event: ~17.5 h, with continuous benchmark load from ~20:00 and ~15 server boots.
- The queue stopped itself (master chain: `Xid present, stop 04:51:29`). No GPU process is left. kvs1 lost4, the k=4 retry and
  the Swift 1.0 control did NOT run.

## Step-0 partial results (before the Xid)

NCCL microbench (nccl-tests v2.20.0 on NCCL 2.29.7, single process `-g 2`, `NCCL_P2P_DISABLE=1`, out-of-place µs):

| bytes | all_reduce default (RING/SIMPLE) | all_reduce LL | LL128 | graph-launched Simple / LL |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 8 KB | 17.1 | 11.5 | 21.3 | 14.9 / 9.2 |
| 16 KB | 18.6 | 14.5 | 22.6 | 17.0 / 12.5 |
| 32 KB | 22.6 | 22.7 | 29.6 | 20.4 / 21.0 |
| 64 KB | 30.8 | 38.8 | 42.3 | 28.9 / 36.7 |

LL is faster only below ~32 KB. One decode token's hidden state is 10 KB (5120 × bf16), so MTP draft steps (1 token) would gain
~5 µs per all-reduce, while the k=3 verify (4 tokens, 40 KB) would not. A single fixed `NCCL_PROTO=LL` is therefore not clearly a
win; a size-dependent choice would be. HyperQwen issue #190's +12% is not confirmed here.
HyperQwen A/B, default arm only (smoke_vllm, 512 new tokens, 2 passes): 98.8 / 94.3 / 94.8 tok/s at depth 512 / 8k / 32k,
step 29.4–30.8 ms. The LL arm did not produce data: the harness's env check flagged a mismatch on LL-1 (it read `NCCL_PROTO` unset
in the worker processes' /proc environ although NCCL logged "set by environment" twice — probably a false alarm), and LL-2 hit the Xid.

## KLD + identity (2026-09-29 04:18–04:41, recomputed on CPU after a scoring fix)

Harness `scratchpad/kld/` (reference: Swift 1.5 Q6_K on llama.cpp upstream, f16 KV; top-64 KL with tail bucket; identical token ids
on all engines). The first aggregate was wrong: it scored system/user/tool-turn tokens, on which chat-tuned models are near one-hot
and chaotic (KLD 1–2 there under any perturbation). Recomputed over assistant-authored and plain-document rows only (3,430 rows;
`out/results-recomputed.md`). Cross-check: `llama-perplexity --kl-divergence` IQ4_XS vs Q6_K on a plain chunk = 0.0089 (top-1 97.1%).

| Arm (vs Swift 1.5 Q6_K) | KLD mean ± se | p99 | top-1 | PPL |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Swift 1.5 Q5_K_M, llama.cpp | 0.0036 ± 0.0002 | 0.042 | 97.9% | 1.954 |
| Swift 1.5 IQ4_XS, llama.cpp (f16 KV; q8_0 KV identical) | 0.0085 ± 0.0005 | 0.097 | 97.0% | 1.965 |
| Swift 1.0 IQ4_XS, llama.cpp [control] | 0.0104 ± 0.0006 | 0.110 | 97.3% | 1.960 |
| Base Qwen3.8 IQ4_XS, llama.cpp [control] | 0.0137 ± 0.0008 | 0.148 | 96.7% | 1.964 |
| **HQ Swift 1.5 W4A16, FP8 KV calibrated (served)** | **0.0235 ± 0.0010** | 0.257 | 94.9% | 1.979 |
| HQ Swift 1.5 W4A16, FP8 KV scale 1.0 | 0.0236 ± 0.0010 | 0.271 | 95.0% | 1.979 |
| HQ Swift 1.0 W4A16, FP8 KV | 0.0265 ± 0.0010 | 0.253 | 94.4% | 1.984 |

Findings:
- **HyperQwen Swift 1.5 is ~2.8× further from the reference than llama.cpp IQ4_XS** (0.0235 vs 0.0085; ~6.5× Q5_K_M). It sits below
  IQ4_XS quality, not at it. Caveat: the vLLM arms include engine numerics (bf16 activations, Marlin, TP reduction), and no arm
  separates engine from quant; the llama arms share the reference's engine. Public data points the same way
  (jedi95/qwen3.8-27b-kld: AWQ-INT4 0.044 vs EXL3 3.5 bpw 0.023, different reference).
- **The calibrated FP8 KV scales are not the problem**: scale 1.0 vs calibrated KL = 0.0012, and both arms score the same vs the
  reference. The kvs1 hypothesis for HQ 1.5's extra tokens is effectively ruled out (kvs1 lost4 run not needed for that).
- q8_0 vs f16 KV on llama.cpp: KL 0.0003 — negligible.
- Identity (thin margins, provisional): Swift 1.0 IQ4_XS and especially base Qwen are further from Swift 1.5 than Swift 1.5 IQ4_XS
  is; HQ 1.0 is closer to Swift 1.0 IQ4_XS than to base Qwen (ratio 1.10); HQ 1.5 is closer to the Swift 1.5 reference than to
  Swift 1.0 (1.18). Consistent with the served checkpoints being the intended Swift versions.
- **Serving hazard:** HQ 1.5 with `--kv-cache-dtype bfloat16` on FlashInfer gives wrong attention (PPL 12.3 vs 2.3): vLLM keeps
  the checkpoint's k/v scales for a bf16 cache and FlashInfer prefill applies them (`compressed_tensors.py:1201`,
  `flashinfer.py:2224`). Do not serve HQ 1.5 with a BF16 KV cache without the scale-1.0 hook. FLASH_ATTN (`CTX=fast`) unverified.
- Next KLD run (after reboot, ~35–40 min): `data2/` with six 8k assistant-heavy rollouts (29.9k scored rows) for tighter identity margins.

### KLD re-run on data2 (2026-09-29 09:28–09:50, post-reboot; 6 × 8k assistant-heavy rollouts, ~30k scored rows)

`scratchpad/kld/out2/results.md`. vs Swift 1.5 Q6_K (llama.cpp, f16 KV):

| Arm | KLD ± se | p99 | top-1 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Swift 1.5 Q5_K_M | 0.0040 ± 0.0001 | 0.044 | 98.2% |
| Swift 1.5 IQ4_XS (f16 KV; q8_0 KV identical) | 0.0109 ± 0.0003 | 0.126 | 97.0% |
| Swift 1.0 IQ4_XS [control] | 0.0142 ± 0.0003 | 0.131 | 96.9% |
| Base Qwen3.8 IQ4_XS [control] | 0.0183 ± 0.0003 | 0.186 | 96.2% |
| HQ Swift 1.5 W4A16, FP8 KV calibrated (served) | 0.0254 ± 0.0005 | 0.288 | 95.2% |
| HQ Swift 1.5 W4A16, FP8 KV scale 1.0 | 0.0256 ± 0.0005 | 0.291 | 95.2% |
| HQ Swift 1.5 W4A16, BF16 KV (scale forced to 1.0) | 0.0250 ± 0.0005 | 0.281 | 95.2% |
| HQ Swift 1.0 W4A16, FP8 KV | 0.0353 ± 0.0006 | 0.349 | 94.2% |

Confirms the first run with tighter errors: HQ 1.5 is ~2.3× IQ4_XS and ~6× Q5_K_M; KV dtype/scales are irrelevant (BF16 vs FP8
KL 0.0011), so the gap is the int4 body (plus vLLM numerics, not separated). Swift 1.5 GPTQ body is clearly better than Swift 1.0's
AWQ body (0.0254 vs 0.0353). Identity: all 5 checks PASS (ratios 1.04–1.26): HQ 1.0 is Swift 1.0, HQ 1.5 is Swift 1.5.

### Swift 1.0 control on the four lost tasks (HyperQwen, 2026-09-29 09:50–10:18)

`campaign-hyperqwen10-lost4-rep{1,2}-20260929`, same config as the Swift 1.5 repeats (u64, min_p, GPU_UTIL 0.86), 4/4 solved each:
rep1 63.5k (book 6.4k, complex 19.1k, react 20.3k, forth 17.8k), rep2 61.5k (book 17.5k, complex 8.1k, react 12.0k, forth 23.9k).
HQ 1.0 now has 4 samples: 49.5k, 42.3k, 63.5k, 61.5k (median 55.5k) vs HQ 1.5 5 samples median 65.5k (mean 66.4k) → ~+18% on
medians, well inside the per-config spread (Q6_K alone spans 42.5k–111.6k). **Conclusion: no demonstrated Swift 1.5 verbosity
regression on HyperQwen**; the earlier +27% came from comparing against 2 low HQ 1.0 draws. Book-store is the one task where
HQ 1.5 still runs high more often (8–26k vs 6–18k).

## Host behaviour after the Xid, and mitigations considered (2026-09-29; user chose not to apply them for now)

Full report: `scratchpad/freeze-investigation.md`. The host did not freeze after the 04:51 Xid 79: kernel, network, sshd and cron
kept working for 4 h 22 min. At 09:13 cosmic-comp (bound to the dead card 1) aborted, the console fell back to fbcon on nvidia-drm
and hung on the dead GPU; the user's 09:14:21 SSH login authenticated but hung in pam_systemd → logind CreateSession. Hard reset
at ~09:15. AER is not implemented on root ports 00:03.x (no aer_* sysfs), so "no AER errors" is not evidence about the GPU links.
Mitigations considered, ranked, NOT applied (user decision 2026-09-29): (1) root journal watcher that reboots on Xid 79/154;
(2) run unattended campaigns headless (`systemctl isolate multi-user.target`); (3) rescue sshd without PAM/logind on the Tailscale IP,
ssh OOMScoreAdjust=-900; (4) `nvidia_drm.fbdev=0`; (5) Gen3 cap on card 1 (00:03.1); (6) hardware: reseat card 1 riser, check
the 4-pin slot feed and PSU; (7) free `/` + journald cap (done: CUDA 12 removed, `/` 12 GB free); (8) kdump/crashkernel,
efi_pstore, panic=10, panic_on_oops=1, optional hung_task_panic; (9) load sp5100_tco so the configured RuntimeWatchdogSec works.
If the SSH session hangs again after a GPU fault: try sysrq `w` (dump blocked tasks) before resetting.

### HyperQwen Swift 1.5 MTP k=4 (2026-09-29 10:35–11:07)

`campaign-hyperqwen15-u16-minp-k4-longtail-20260929`: DRAFT_TOKENS=4, `--prefix-match-unit 16` (KV block 848 at k=4), GPU_UTIL 0.78,
CUDA_HOME=/usr/local/cuda-13.2 (fresh FlashInfer JIT cache). 8/8 solved, 1749 s, 151.7k output tokens, prefill 213 s,
**decode 105.0 tok/s vs 94.4 at k=3 (+11%)**, acceptance 64.0% vs 70.1%. Wall is not comparable (token volume differs: 151.7k vs
141.3k); decode tok/s is the metric. Single run. Unit 16 vs 64 changes prefix reuse granularity only.

Comparison of the HyperQwen eight-task runs (prefix reuse = cached_tokens / input_tokens summed over model-calls.jsonl):

| Run | Wall s | Out tok | In tok | Cached | Uncached | Prefill s | Prefill tok/s | Decode s | Decode tok/s | Accept |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| HQ 1.0 min_p, unit 832, k=3 | 1371 | 102.9k | 2.09M | 87.2% | 267k | 281 | 951 | 1013 | 101.5 | 71.6% |
| HQ 1.0 min_p, u64, k=3 | 1396 | 107.0k | 2.47M | 92.2% | 193k | 208 | 927 | 1095 | 97.8 | 68.7% |
| HQ 1.5 base, u64, k=3 | 1825 | 141.3k | 3.81M | 94.4% | 216k | 230 | 938 | 1498 | 94.4 | 70.1% |
| HQ 1.5 chunk4096, u64, k=3 | 1599 | 120.7k | 2.85M | 92.6% | 211k | 230 | 915 | 1265 | 95.5 | 71.7% |
| HQ 1.5 k=4, u16 | 1749 | 151.7k | 3.80M | 94.8% | 197k | 213 | 925 | 1445 | 105.0 | 64.0% |

Reading: prefill throughput is flat (~915–950 tok/s) in every configuration, so prefill time tracks uncached tokens, which track the
trajectory. u16 reuses about as well as u64 (94.8% vs 94.4%); both beat the 832 block (87%). k=4 is the only change that moved
decode (+11%) despite lower acceptance (64% vs 70%). Wall differences are dominated by output-token volume (103k–152k), i.e. noise.
The unit must divide the KV block: 832 at k=3 (64 works), 848 at k=4 (64 fails; 16, 53, 106, 212, 424 divide). Correction 12:00: unit 4 was never observed to fail; vllm#55766 affects only the V1 runner (see SP/vllm-u4-bug.md).

## swift-mtp-fast arms (2026-09-29 11:56–12:10; `smoke/mtp-fast/*.json`, 2 passes, Swift 1.5 IQ4_XS, split 50,50, 512 new tokens)

tok/s (change vs U, draft acceptance):

|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | 512 | 71.9 (0.55) | 69.7 (−3%) | **85.6 (+19%, 0.78)** | 62.8 (−13%, 0.48) |
| 3 | 8192 | 73.8 (0.64) | 73.2 (−1%) | **83.3 (+13%, 0.80)** | 60.6 (−18%, 0.48) |
| 2 | 8192 | 75.7 (0.84) | 79.1 (+4%) | 79.3 (+5%, 0.86) | 65.3 (−14%, 0.62) |
| 1 | 8192 | 58.4 (0.80) | 58.1 | 63.2 (+8%, 0.92) | **66.0 (+13%, 0.93)** |

- P (axis ACC): probabilistic drafting + rejection verify raises acceptance from 0.64 to 0.80 at k=3; the best config is P at k=3.
  B0 = U confirms the branch is neutral with the new paths off.
- PF (axis STEP): at k=1 it is +13% with P's acceptance (one MTP decode saved per round, as designed). At k ≥ 2 acceptance
  collapses (0.48 at k=3), so the port conditions later draft positions wrongly — a bug in 3ec79cbfa, not a property of the idea.
- GPU sampling arms (PG/PGT/PGTF) crashed on CUDA; fixed on `swift-mtp-fast-gs`, not yet run.

  reduces to greedy at temp 0. The temp-1 first-token distribution test was uninformative: the prompt had one possible first
  token (400/400). So P's distribution at temp 1 is exact by construction (rejection sampling) but not empirically tested yet.

## Branch fixes after the arms (2026-09-29 12:00–12:40; CPU only, not yet run on the GPUs)

**GPU sampling under `-sm tensor` crash (axis STEP; our commit 6b8076d96, relates to issue #27467).**
- Symptom: arm PG aborted on the first MTP draft decode, `ggml-backend-meta.cpp:876: GGML_ASSERT(src_ss[i].axis != GGML_BACKEND_SPLIT_AXIS_UNKNOWN)`.
- Cause: the MTP catch-up decode adds rows with `logits=0`, so `n_outputs=0` and `t_logits` is empty. The meta backend returns
  UNKNOWN for zero-element tensors, and `build_sampling` pads `t_logits` unconditionally. Upstream never reached this path
  because it refuses backend samplers under tensor split. The same crash would hit `-bs` on prompt ubatches without outputs.
- Fix: branch `swift-mtp-fast-gs`, 12160c24f (skip TP backend sampling when the ubatch has no outputs) and a4fc87ed9 (a meta-buffer
  view fix that only enables the CPU test). New CPU test `test-tp-backend-sampling` aborts before and passes after.
- Swift 1.5's MTP head uses `model.output` (split on axis 1), not `nextn.shared_head_head`, so admitting GPU sampling was correct.

**Fused catch-up acceptance collapse (axis ACC via STEP; our port 3ec79cbfa of ik_llama.cpp #1736).**
- Symptom: PF acceptance equal to P at k=1, but 0.62 vs 0.86 at k=2 and 0.48 vs 0.80 at k=3.
- Cause: in masked nextn mode `llama_context::decode` copies only the first `n_outputs` rows of `t_h_nextn`, but the qwen35 MTP head
  sets `t_h_nextn` before the out_ids `get_rows`, so it holds all batch rows. With the fused first step `[kept rows…, draft row]`,
  the next draft step read the hidden state of the first kept row. ik_llama.cpp avoids it by reading the last output row explicitly.
  The same latent bug is in upstream's qwen35moe, deepseek2, bailingmoe3, cohere2moe and hy-v3 MTP heads.
- Fix: branch `swift-mtp-fast-fuse` (on top of `-gs`), 2b012f5b4: set `t_h_nextn` after the get_rows in masked mode (6 heads).
- CPU reproduction on Qwen3.5-4B (one nextn layer), ~190 tokens per cell: PF acceptance k=2 0.637 → 0.779, k=3 0.518 → 0.701
  (P 0.729 / 0.651). Non-fused acceptance is byte-identical before and after.
- Also found (unfixed, same for P and PF): the server's checkpoint-restore path (`server-context.cpp` ~4000) returns without
  `common_speculative_accept`, so each partial-accept replay writes one MTP KV row with the wrong hidden state.

## vLLM exact top-20 sampling patch (axis STEP; 2026-09-29, CPU only; `SP/vllm-sampling/`)

- Cause of the 1.6 ms/step: the V2 runner's `RejectionSampler._verify` → `apply_top_k_top_p(logits, k, p)` passes no `k_max`, so the
  HyperQwen small-k patch (`sampler-small-topk-fast-softmax.patch`) never runs, and vLLM's Triton `_topk_topp_kernel` runs with
  grid = rows (4 programs, 4 of 36 SMs) doing serial sweeps over 248,320 logits. With min_p 0.05, usually ≤ k tokens remain finite,
  which sends it down an extra full-vocab top-p branch.
- FlashInfer's sampler cannot replace it: it returns token ids, but the rejection sampler needs the truncated dense distribution;
  HyperQwen also disables it (`VLLM_USE_FLASHINFER_SAMPLER=0`, JIT).
- Patch `fast-topk.patch` (`VLLM_FAST_TOPK=1`): stage 1 tiled top-k per 4096 logits (ties to the lowest index), stage 2 per-row merge
  with min_p (bit-identical threshold), top-k and top-p, scattered back into the dense row. Falls back for top_k > 256, logprobs,
  non-CUDA.
- Exactness on CPU (Triton interpreter): equal to the definition on 7 cases × 2 seeds; 0/80 rows differ from vLLM's own kernel,
  including heavy ties. Estimated saving −1.6 ms/step (~5%). GPU test and serving A/B on machine C.
- Sampling only on TP rank 0 would save nothing: both ranks' kernels overlap by 1.70 ms in the step 0 trace.

## vLLM host-staged AllReduce, second iteration (axis COMM; CPU only; `SP/vllm-ar/`)

- The measured curve (fixed ~6.5 us + ~0.3 us/KiB) shows the write pass and the read pass run in series. A pipelined kernel
  (`VLLM_HOST_STAGED_AR_VARIANT=pipe`, per-chunk 64-bit flags, in-place calls via a temporary) is built; correctness and speed on
  GPUs pending (machine C).
- Expected serving gain 1.0–1.6 ms/step (3–5%): 129 × 40 KiB + 9 × 10 KiB all-reduces per step; the in-model NCCL p50 (24.9 us at
  40 KiB) gives the low end.
- Not addressed: the 6.9 all-gathers per step (0.3–0.6 ms) and the GPU0/GPU1 imbalance (1.8 ms/step).

## vllm#47123 placement review (axis REL; `SP/vllm-u4/placement-review.md`)

- The row test decides correctness, not the placement. The reviewer's placement (inside `_is_uniform_decode`) is slightly better if
  the fact arrives as an explicit `all_rows_decode` argument from `execute_model`; reading runner state there would see the previous
  step's verify mask.
- #47123 misses a resumed request whose 1+k tail holds output tokens, and over-blocks scheduler-padded rows. Our
  `fix-in-is-uniform.patch` covers both (CPU tests 6/6). Upstream main after #51565 may have the same mechanism without spec decode
  (1-token prompt in an all-decode batch) — code reading only. Not posted upstream (user decision 2026-09-29).

## 2026-09-29 16:00 — Root cause of the wrong tokens under TP GPU sampling (fix agent, CPU-verified)

Not an id-mapping bug. `build_sampling` packs (logit, vocab id as F32) per device, and `ggml_backend_meta_gather` all-gathers them as zero-padded slices summed by an AllReduce. On CUDA with NCCL off, that goes through `ggml_cuda_ar_allreduce` (`ggml/src/ggml-cuda/allreduce.cu:764`), which rounds every F32 AllReduce through BF16 (`GGML_CUDA_AR_BF16_THRESHOLD` default 1, allreduce.cu:435). With NCCL on, the same happens above 32768 elements. BF16 keeps 8 significant bits → ids up to 248k come back as other tokens.
The CPU test missed it because CPU devices use the exact generic fallback, and its 128-token vocab is exact in BF16.
Fix `92dc59bd4` (`swift-mtp-fast-gs2`; cherry-picked as `b64d8d884` fuse2 and `453b44133` grammar2): a gather at the end of a subgraph always uses the exact `allreduce_fallback`; other AllReduces are unchanged.
Also fixed: `-bs` + top-p < 1 load abort (top-k with existing candidates returned [1,k]; now reshaped 1-d), and backend greedy returned the candidate index instead of the vocab id.
Test: `test-llama-archs --tp-sampling` (CPU) and `--tp-sampling-gpu` (2 CUDA devices), 40960-token vocab, 4-row ubatch, TV distance ≤ 0.08 (measured 0.021–0.038).
Side note (not measured): the CUDA internal AllReduce rounds model activations through BF16 by default. This is an upstream numerics choice that may affect KLD on -sm tensor; worth a KLD check with GGML_CUDA_AR_BF16_THRESHOLD set high.
GPU check `SP/trackL/gs2_gpu_check.sh` running for gs2, fuse2, grammar2 (log `SP/trackL/gs2_gpu_check.all.log`).
