Findings (axis FID×FWD):
- Group size is the main lever; int8 on "sensitive" tensors helps more per byte on top of g32 than on top of g128. g32 on the sensitive set only is worth nothing (0.0242).
- No vLLM-servable arm reaches IQ4_XS; the closest (0.0133) costs +1.5 GB/GPU and +15% step. Int8 q/k/v/o on g32 (0.0159, +8%) is the best KLD per ms.
- Fused modules (qkv_proj, gate/up, in_proj_qkv/z) must share one scheme. llm-compressor 0.14 removed actorder=group, so act-order g32 was skipped.
- The UkisAI INT4 remote KLD (0.0250) equals the local HQ BF16-KV arm → HQ's int8 lm_head/embed cost ~0 KLD.
Checkpoints (BF16 heads + MTP; need HyperQwen prepare/ before serving): /mnt/scratch/work/models/Swift-1.5-Qwen3.8-27B-W4A16-{g32,g32-attn-int8,g32-o-ssmout-int8,g32-mixed-int8}/ with RECIPE.txt and SHA256SUMS.txt. Machine B destroyed 15:48, ~$3.28. Credit $3.65 (machine D running).

## 2026-09-29 15:50 — GPU check of the TP sampling fix (gs2, fuse2, grammar2): ALL PASSED — SP/trackL/gs2_gpu_check.all.log

- `test-llama-archs --tp-sampling-gpu` OK on 2 CUDA devices (TV 0.038); test-backend-ops TOP_K/GET_ROWS/PAD pass.
- `-bs` with top-p 0.95 now loads; chat text correct ("The capital of France is Paris.", coherent 3-sentence prose), acceptance 0.72–0.81.
- PG (draft GPU sampling + probabilistic) k=3: acceptance 358/459 (512) and 360/452 (8k) — **bit-identical to arm P** (CPU draft sampling). Expected: the backend chain is only top_k(10) (`common/speculative.cpp:1481`), and the host draws from the same 10 candidates with the same RNG. So the fix is exact.
- Speed: PG tg 8k 83.0 / 84.9 / 86.8 (gs2/fuse2/grammar2) vs P 83.3 → 0 to +4%, within noise. Axis STEP: moving the draft sampling to the GPU removes only the 1 MB logits copy; the host sync per draft step remains, and that sync (not the copy) makes the ~13.5 ms/round idle (L0b nsys).
- PGT (+ target `-bs`) = PG in the smoke: smoke uses /completion, which falls back to CPU ("first active sampler is top-k … using CPU"). Target GPU sampling was exercised only by the chat test.
Next lever for llama.cpp: remove the host round trip per draft step (run the k draft steps on the device without a sync, the way vLLM does), not the sampling location.

## 2026-09-29 16:10 — r4: why llama.cpp samples first and checks the grammar after (grammar_first=false) — SP/grammar-committee/r4-fastpath.txt

- Why: PR #4306 (kalomaze, 2023-12) for speed: 15 → 1 ms/token on a 32k vocab. ggerganov (#8643) and JohannesGaessler (#12643) noted that it is not the masked distribution, and it was kept for speed. Nobody measured it.
- Law: P_f = (1−ε)·ĉ + ε·m, TV(P_f, m) = (1−ε)·TV(ĉ, m) (verified on 2e5 random cases); worst case for our sampler TV ≈ 0.24 at one position. MTP rejection reproduces P_f exactly.
- Measured (teacher forcing on codex rollouts, real lazy GBNF): Qwen3.5-4B proxy 27,693 grammar-active positions, ε > 0 at 17 (0.06%, choice points such as the function name), one position TV 0.039, 0 argmax changes. Swift 1.5 IQ4_XS: 2,043 positions, ε > 0 at 0. Grammar-active positions: 91% free text, 6% choice, 3% forced. Tool-call validity cannot be affected.
- Cost of exactness: GBNF full mask p50 51.5 ms (free text) → grammar_first=true would cost ~170–200 ms per grammar-active round, decode −45%. llguidance mask ~2.9 ms (+~5% overall), but it is blocked (no lazy triggers in the %llguidance branch, chat handler emits GBNF).
- Nearly free exact variant (~15 lines in common_sampler_sample): check every token of S (mean |S| 1.06–1.16) and the top_p edge condition; fall back to the masked resample otherwise (fired on 0.24% of Swift positions, ~0.15 ms). Sound in 37,597 random cases.
Verdict (axis REL/FID): the fast path is fine for codex quality; not worth fixing. The exact variant is a clean contract if we ever need one for GPU top-K candidates.

## 2026-09-29 16:54 — Machine D: patched HQ stack k sweep, two concurrent requests, headless imbalance — SP/vast-hq-d/

Patched = g128 + host-staged AR + fast-topk. Step ms precise (sd ≤ 0.12); per-boot tok/s noisy (sd up to 15), so k is compared on modelled tok/s from pooled per-position acceptance (0.792/0.614/0.482/0.370/0.282/0.246).
- Each extra draft token costs 1.4–1.6 ms/step. Patches cut 2.6–2.9 ms/step at k=4 (−8.3 to −8.6%, same as C).
- Modelled tok/s at 8k: unpatched k=4 104.7; patched k=3 106.9, k=4 114.5 (+9%), k=5 116.9 (+12%), k=6 119.0 (+14%). At 32k: 101.3 / 102.9 / 110.2 / 110.9 / 113.0. k=5 vs k=4 ≈ +2.5% and k=6 vs k=5 ≈ +2%, both within boot noise. k=6 block 864, KV pool 243k vs 255k at k=4.
- Two concurrent requests (8k / 32k, aggregate tok/s): unpatched k=5 209.7 / 196.7; patched k=5 205.2 / 195.3 (step −2.1/−1.9 ms but lower acceptance in those boots, 51–54% vs 56–57%, ~2–3 SEM); patched k=4 210.8 / 187.4. At batch 2, k=4 ≥ k=5. The AR patch is correct at batch-2 sizes (80–120 KiB, 1020 checks), but near break-even with NCCL there (C: 64 KiB 24.7 vs 26.2 µs, 128 KiB 46 vs 39 µs) → most batch-2 gain is fast-topk.
- Headless imbalance: GPU0 vs GPU1 Marlin 19.79 vs 19.78 ms/step (71.82 vs 71.79 µs/kernel), clocks 2760/2790 MHz, no throttle reasons. The local +1.8 ms/step on GPU0 is not rank-0 work → supports the display (compositor on GPU0) hypothesis. Note: under llama.cpp the local GPU0 GEMV gap is only +1.7%, so the display seems to hurt vLLM's Marlin/graph pattern more (unexplained).
Recommendation: serve profile k=4 (unit 16) for 2 agents; k=5 only for single-agent use. Cost ~$0.84, destroyed 16:53; 0 instances left; credit $3.14.

## 2026-09-29 17:30 — CORRECTION to the L0b nsys section (15:37): graph re-instantiation and idle were averages over startup

- All 526 cudaGraphInstantiate fall in the first ~0.86 s of the 4.04 s capture: the prompt re-eval of 4 tokens (258 per-device subgraphs), the first catch-up and draft, and the second verify. Every instantiate follows a failed cudaGraphExecUpdate. After 0.86 s, rounds are pure replay (0 captures). So there is **no per-round re-instantiation**. It is a one-time ~0.7 s cost (TTFT + first round). Likely cause (inferred from code, not proven): the cache key is `cgraph->nodes[0]`, which under -sm tensor lives in a meta context that is reset on rebuild (`ggml-backend-meta.cpp` stc_compute rotation).
- #28652 is an issue (alternating variants on one key); its patch, PR #28666, was closed unmerged and does not split our collision (the prompt re-eval and the verify both have n_tokens 4). Not useful for us. Analysis: SP/trackL/graphkey/.
- Steady state (after 0.9 s): median round ~41 ms (verify 28.4 ms); GPU idle 23% per GPU; ~350 gaps > 0.2 ms in ~70 rounds (~4.6 per round, mean 1.75 ms) = **~8–9 ms idle per round** (not 13.5).

## 2026-09-29 ~18:00 — Local eight-task HQ k=4 u16 with host-staged AR + fast top-k (overlay SP/vllm-both/)

8/8 solved. Decode 126.6 vs 105.0 tok/s (+20.6%); step ms (decode_s / verify steps) 29.20 vs 33.88 (−13.8%); per-task step 28.80–29.38 vs 32.18–35.07 (no overlap). Acceptance 67.6% vs 64.0% (tokens/step 3.70 vs 3.56, consistent with 1 + 4·acc). I recomputed the tok/s from SUMMARY.json (88,255/696.8 and 151,710/1445.3).
Both patches were proven active before the run (serve log: HOST_STAGED backend lines; JIT log: fast-topk _candidates/_select kernels; manifest env).
Caveats (provisional): N=1 per arm, run sequentially (the baseline ran this morning, while the desktop was in use). The acceptance difference is run noise (fast top-k keeps the same candidates). Lower token volume means shallower mean depth, which shortens steps slightly (est. ≤ 0.3 ms; not measured). The step here includes scheduler/host time, so it is not the same measure as the C/D smoke step. Why the local cut (−4.7 ms) exceeds C/D (−2.6 to −2.9 ms) is unverified; the hypothesis is costlier NCCL locally (GPU1 at Gen3 x8, display on GPU0).
To firm up: one interleaved repeat (unpatched, patched) on the same afternoon, ~50 min GPU.

## 2026-09-29 ~18:05 — Why agent traffic gains less from P, and the per-call fixed cost (CPU analysis) — SP/agentic-gap/REPORT.txt

- P's whole effect on agent traffic is acceptance: tokens/round 3.248 vs 3.102 (+4.7%). ms/round 41.90 vs 42.93 (−2.4%) is context mix and run noise (U rounds ~1.1k tokens deeper; one U binary spans 70.2–76.4 tok/s over 4 runs). A pooled per-call fit finds no P effect on per-round or per-call cost.
- The smoke's +16% was one 512-token draw; the same content reordered gave +7.1%. Codex output has lower entropy: reasoning (66–73% of tokens) +7% tokens/round, tool calls already near the maximum 4.0, prose ~0. The gain is flat across depth (10k–99k) and output length. Rejected: depth, short generations, catch-up in eval time (it counts as prompt time), untimed work (1.3 ms median).
- CORRECTION: the "fixed 160–470 ms per call inside eval time" (r3 regression) is a fitting artifact. The robust eval-side intercept is ~25–90 ms, mostly grammar-active rounds in short tool replies.
- The real fixed cost is on the prompt side: follow-up prompt_ms = 284 ± 48 ms + 1.19 ms/new token + 13.6 ms per 1k context (~540 ms at the median follow-up). Every sub-chunk prompt batch costs ~200 ms. Follow-ups split into 2–3 batches at checkpoint offsets (user-message start, end−132, end−4), and `create_checkpoint` (server-context.cpp:2353, the same in upstream at :2375) runs before each. Each checkpoint copies 150 MiB of GDN state into a fresh std::vector via a synchronous pageable D2H, plus the whole MTP draft KV (`llama_kv_cache::state_write` ignores PARTIAL_ONLY, llama-kv-cache.cpp:2061). CPU-measured: a fresh 150 MiB resize costs 76–119 ms (glibc mmap + page faults); with `GLIBC_TUNABLES=glibc.malloc.mmap_max=0` 22–42 ms.
- Per eight-task campaign (P): acceptance ceiling ~95 s; prompt fixed cost 46 s (~25–40 s recoverable); template/tokenize 6–8 s; eval-side per call 4–8 s. Fixes: glibc tunables (no code, est. 10–25 s), skip the draft KV dump / honour PARTIAL_ONLY (small patch, est. ~20 s), pinned buffer pool or LLAMA_STATE_SEQ_FLAGS_ON_DEVICE (larger). Do not use --ctx-checkpoints 0 (19 of 604 follow-ups needed a checkpoint).
- Not verified on the GPU. Probe ready: SP/agentic-gap/gpu_ckpt_probe.sh (4–5 min; arms P, P + glibc tunables, P + --ctx-checkpoints 0).

(2) Sync-free premise: steady idle 8.9 ms/round, of which the launch thread spends only ~2.3 ms/round in CUDA calls; ~6.5 ms/round is host CPU code outside CUDA calls. "It is the sync" rested on one indirect datum.

|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| unpatched original | 151.7k | 3.80M | 105.0 | 64.0% | 33.88 | 25.7k |
| unpatched rep2 | 120.3k | 3.69M | 108.4 | 64.2% | 32.86 | 23.7k |
| patched original | 88.3k | 1.84M | 126.6 | 67.6% | 29.20 | 21.0k |
| patched rep2 | 173.7k | 3.81M | 128.5 | 70.1% | 29.58 | 27.4k |
All 8/8. Pooled step 33.43 → 29.45 ms (−11.9%); rep2 pair alone −3.28 ms (−10.0%), even though the patched rep2 ran deeper. Equal-depth fits: ~−3.7 to −4 ms at 10–30k. Every patched task is faster than every unpatched task. The unpatched rep2 ran with no overlay (plain venv).
Acceptance is higher with the patches in both pairs (+3.6 and +5.9 pp). Hypothesis (not verified): vLLM's Triton top-k/top-p path is inexact (keeps all ties at k=1, cuts top-p up to ~2% off, can exceed k), which broadens the target law; the exact path samples the intended law, so rejection verify accepts more. This conflicts with machine D's batch-2 result (patched acceptance lower), so it needs a check: compare acceptance per boot in a smoke with VLLM_FAST_TOPK on/off, host-staged AR held constant. Until then, credit the patches only with the step-ms gain (~12%); decode tok/s rose ~19%.

## 2026-09-29 19:33 — GPU checkpoint probe (queue item 10, `SP/agentic-gap/gpu_ckpt_probe.sh`, log `SP/queue/logs/10-ckpt-probe.log`)

Swift 1.5 IQ4_XS, campaign-P flags. A 201-token follow-up prompt. One boot per arm; each number is the median over the follow-up requests of that boot.

| Arm | prompt_ms at 8k | prompt_ms at 24k |
|---|---:|---:|
| A default | 922 | 1446 |
| B A + glibc heap tunables | 1151 | 1157 |
| C A + `--ctx-checkpoints 0` | 415 | 550 |

Checkpoints are 181/220/260 MiB (min/median/max, 40 per arm). Removing them (C) cuts 507 ms at 8k and 896 ms at 24k from each follow-up, and the cost grows with depth. This confirms the CPU estimate (~540 ms per follow-up) on the GPU. The glibc tunables (B) are mixed: worse at 8k and better at 24k. With one boot per arm, B is inconclusive. C is an upper bound and is unsafe for agents (diverging requests then need a full re-prefill). The code fix (agent, branch `swift-mtp-fast-ckpt`, queue item 50) is the real test.
